:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       442c807f-c9d9-45a7-88bf-d38cbbca09cf
:END:
#+title: Parse
#+lilac_maxListLevel: 4

* Overview
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       e16dd5c7-0a63-4c52-afe9-744fdf853643
:END:

We use [[https://github.com/mrkkrp/megaparsec][Megaparsec]] to parse an Org document. The parsing requires us to read in
everything in a single Org file.

We use a flat *list of /cells/* [fn:cell] [[(data:OrgDoc-cells)]] for parsing, where each
cell can be a

  - [[id:16a68a74-d4cc-41a4-ac77-cbca77de2a31][heading]] (aka [[https://orgmode.org/manual/Headlines.html][headlines]]),

  - [[id:272567ab-4077-4a47-ae78-dfe77243ccc0][paragraph]] ([[id:c8616126-215d-49be-8874-c2aca7d93a54][prose]] text, made up of mainly [[id:b616c1e2-6b42-41cf-8e5d-c1832f95b59b][Styled text]] and [[id:f8254d58-7a3d-4595-a265-e0d6255e6ab5][Links]]
    including [[id:da4aa2a7-3896-4969-aa5e-5e3d842c35db][Line labels]], [[id:4127feee-755d-430e-8fef-192e95143790][Footnotes]], and [[id:a54a7762-a861-42b1-b2c8-2b760281052c][Citations]]),

  - ordered or unordered [[id:58f69993-599d-4389-ba47-3056b1bb2350][list item]] (can be nested),

  - [[id:cae993e5-02bd-4f89-a6d3-20b41adaff8f][block]] (code block, quotation, example, etc),

  - [[id:d0f60631-8b30-4ae1-992d-49c7b0ea38fb][table]],

  - [[id:a090bf9c-0e52-422b-b0f0-51e3b1bf1698][figure]] (image),

  - LaTeX math fragment ([[id:216e94b7-e037-4f3e-856a-2040d0d0a75a][inline]] or [[id:d1064535-5c98-4c80-a901-5cdc1fd5297f][standalone]]),

  - [[id:4127feee-755d-430e-8fef-192e95143790][footnote]], or

  - [[id:a54a7762-a861-42b1-b2c8-2b760281052c][citation]].

This list-of-cells scheme is good for quickly making sense of the overall
document structure in linear fashion, start to finish. It also simplifies the
data structure --- we just parse one cell after the other. Though using a tree
data structure for the headings could be useful for situations where tree-based
manipulations are desirable, we will never need to do such manipulations for
purposes of weaving the Org input into HTML (or any other format). Hence our
preference for arranging the cells as a list instead of a tree.

In summary an Org document is made up of cells, as well as some metadata
[[(data:OrgDoc-meta)]] (such as the title, author name, etc). The doc also has an OID
[[(data:OrgDoc-oid)]], which allows other Org files to refer to it (even if the Org file
does not have any headings, which are the only other things that can have OIDs).

** ~OrgDoc~ data structure
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       5df15f17-c630-4ddf-bade-66084c430a07
:END:

The ~OrgDoc~ is important because it's what we end up with after we're done
parsing a raw Org file.

#+name: data:OrgDoc
#+begin_src haskell
type OrgDoc :: Type
data OrgDoc = OrgDoc
  { oid :: OID, -- (ref:data:OrgDoc-oid)
    meta :: Map Text Text, -- (ref:data:OrgDoc-meta)
    cells :: [Cell], -- (ref:data:OrgDoc-cells)
    pseudoEdges :: [PseudoEdge], -- (ref:data:OrgDoc-pseudoEdges)
    footnoteRefs :: Map Text (Int, LCI), -- (ref:data:OrgDoc-footnoteRefs)
    glossary :: Map Text LCI, -- (ref:data:OrgDoc-glossary)
    lineLabels :: Map Text Int, -- (ref:data:OrgDoc-lineLabels)
    cslPath :: Maybe FilePath, -- (ref:data:OrgDoc-cslPath)
    bibPaths :: [FilePath], -- (ref:data:OrgDoc-bibPaths)
    citations :: [Citation] -- (ref:data:OrgDoc-citations)
  }
  deriving stock (Eq, Ord, Generic, Show)
  deriving anyclass (FromJSON, ToJSON) -- (ref:deriving-anyclass)
#+end_src

As stated earlier, we use a list of cells [[(data:OrgDoc-cells)]] to represent most of the
document content. The ~Int~ value we use to index into this list of cells is given
its own type [[newtype:LCI]] to avoid using the ~Int~ primitive (using ~Int~ everywhere
can get confusing).

#+name: newtype:LCI
#+begin_src haskell
type LCI :: Type
newtype LCI = LCI
  { unLCI :: Int
  }
  deriving stock (Eq, Ord, Generic, Show)
  deriving anyclass (FromJSON, FromJSONKey, ToJSON)
#+end_src

The ~oid~ [[(data:OrgDoc-oid)]] is a unique identifier for the document. Every Org file we
parse is required to have a

#+begin_src txt
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       xxxxxxxx-xxxx-xxxx-xxxx-xxxxxxxxxxxx
:END:
#+end_src

at the beginning of the document to uniquely identify it. It is parsed into an
~OID~ type, which just wraps around a UUID. This way we can be agnostic about the
concrete details of a UUID in the rest of the program.

#+name: newtype:OID
#+begin_src haskell
type OID :: Type
newtype OID = OID UUID
  deriving stock (Eq, Ord, Generic)
  deriving anyclass (FromJSON, FromJSONKey, ToJSON)
#+end_src

There are some helpers in [[id:5908cefc-0e82-41a5-84ac-1c8c826f5e62][Org ID (OID)]] for this type, which plays a big role in
Lilac because OIDs are also used for headings; OIDs for documents and headings
form the building block of Org links between them.

The ~meta~ field [[(data:OrgDoc-meta)]] captures all document metadata, in a simple
key/value map. One example is ~#+title: ...~ used to specify the human-friendly
title of the document.

The ~deriving anyclass (ToJSON)~ [[(deriving-anyclass)]] is needed to create the
default implementation for JSON conversion automatically for us. It is the
equivalent of writing a default instance like

#+begin_src haskell
instance ToJSON OrgDoc
#+end_src

but it's shorter because we don't have to put it on its own line, and it's even
more closely coupled with the declaration of the ~OrgDoc~ type itself. If we try
to use ~deriving stock (Eq, Generic, Show, ToJSON)~ we get an error like

#+begin_quote
‘ToJSON’ is not a stock derivable class (Eq, Show, etc.)
#+end_quote

which is why we use the ~deriving anyclass~ variant. All child types also require
the same ~deriving anyclass (ToJSON)~ declaration for this to work. At the end of
it though, we can use the ~encode~ and ~encodeFile~ functions from ~Data.Aeson~.

** Auxiliary data structures

We also need several other auxiliary data structures to encode various bits of
information beyond just what the cells have in them. Basically all of these
auxiliary bits encode information about relationships /between/ certain cells.

*** ~pseudoEdges~
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       3dcdfd21-5e2e-45bf-90bd-3a6a919724c5
:END:

The ~pseudoEdges~ [[(data:OrgDoc-pseudoEdges)]] are edges that are ultimately used to create
a graph representation of links between cells (and ultimately they are finalized
in [[id:a1f2c3b3-9d61-4630-a52e-a38d0a4ef1dc::f:finalizeEdges][f:finalizeEdges]] and used to generate backlinks at [[id:a1f2c3b3-9d61-4630-a52e-a38d0a4ef1dc::f:mkBacklinks][f:mkBacklinks]]). In that
final representation, we want to use global cell index numbers to represent the
universe of all cells in a [[id:25827c17-2015-434b-b686-db3d72b2ee4d::data:Archive][data:Archive]]. However, inside the parser for a single
Org document, we don't have access to this global cell index number, as that is
computed separately in [[id:fb503792-b075-4bd2-a215-8e64b9ee6cb4::f:mkArchive][f:mkArchive]] (in other words, this computation depends on
what other Org files (as objects) are included in the archive). Also, we don't
know the target cell's number either, and the best information we have is just a
[[id:f8254d58-7a3d-4595-a265-e0d6255e6ab5::data:LinkOid][data:LinkOid]]. Because of these issues, we call the edges here "pseudo" edges. To
represent these edges, we use a ~newtype~.

#+name: newtype:PseudoEdge
#+begin_src haskell
type PseudoEdge :: Type
newtype PseudoEdge = PseudoEdge (LCI, LinkOid)
  deriving stock (Eq, Ord, Generic, Show)
  deriving anyclass (FromJSON, ToJSON)
#+end_src

The [[newtype:PseudoEdge]] represents a link between a local cell index and a target
cell via the [[id:f8254d58-7a3d-4595-a265-e0d6255e6ab5::data:LinkOid][data:LinkOid]]. Both of these values are immediately available to us,
so that's why we use them for the pseudo edge representation (there's no need to
compute these values lazily after the fact when there is more information
available).

*** ~footnoteRefs~

Footnotes get their own separate data structure called ~footnoteRefs~
[[(data:OrgDoc-footnoteRefs)]], because we have to remember the order of when these
footnotes are referenced from other cells (anywhere we could have prose, such as
paragraphs, headings, etc). This ordering is needed to determine which number to
display as a superscript when creating the link to the footnote definition. This
same ordering information is used in [[id:891f2674-2be9-4e63-b0f8-7ca43f1354fb::f:weaveObject][f:weaveObject]] which rearranges the footnote
definitions so that they are woven in the order they are referenced, not the
order in which they are defined (parsed). This is unlike every other cell where
the order of parsing them (cell-by-cell) is preserved during weaving.

*** ~glossary~

For the ~glossary~ [[(data:OrgDoc-glossary)]], see [[id:73acf466-6941-4c3b-9d6d-e0843d9b0480][Description lists, aka glossary terms]]. We
save all glossary terms we find for this document, so that we can print them out
later, much like citations.

*** ~lineLabels~

The ~lineLabels~ [[(data:OrgDoc-lineLabels)]] are for [[id:da4aa2a7-3896-4969-aa5e-5e3d842c35db][Line labels]] (named positions /inside/ a
code block, which you can link to from the prose text). We reset the numbering
of these back to 1 every time we see a new code block, so we keep track of the
assigned numbers here with a map.

*** Citations
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       a72a7d6a-a127-4233-b67a-3800961ef766
:END:

The ~cslPath~ [[(data:OrgDoc-cslPath)]] is the path to the citation style file we want to
use for generating citations (for all of the citations used in the ~OrgDoc~).

The ~bibPaths~ [[(data:OrgDoc-bibPaths)]] is the list of files that each have a list of
bibliographical works that we want to cite. This is a list of files, and not
just a single list, because we want to allow users to be able to use multiple
files in case they want to reference a work already captured in a separate file
somewhere else (essentially amounting to file reuse).

Finally the ~citations~ [[(data:OrgDoc-citations)]] are the stylized citations used by the
~OrgDoc~. See [[id:a54a7762-a861-42b1-b2c8-2b760281052c][Citations]] for details.

** Top-level parser ~parseOrgDoc~

The top-level parsing function is [[f:parseOrgDoc]], which takes some raw text input
and parses it into an ~OrgDoc~ ([[id:5df15f17-c630-4ddf-bade-66084c430a07::data:OrgDoc][data:OrgDoc]]), using the [[f:orgDocParser]] as the main
workhorse [[(main-combinator-orgDocParser)]].

#+name: f:parseOrgDoc
#+begin_src haskell
parseOrgDoc :: Text -> Either Text OrgDoc
parseOrgDoc input = case MP.runParser
  (runStateT orgDocParser defaultOrgParserState) -- (ref:main-combinator-orgDocParser)
  ""
  input of
  Left err -> Left . T.pack $ MP.errorBundlePretty err
  Right (orgDoc, _pState) -> Right orgDoc
#+end_src

The [[f:orgDocParser]] is itself composed of other parser combinators. The
hierarchy of the main parsers look like this:

  - [[f:parseOrgDoc]]

      - [[f:orgDocParser]]

          - [[f:cellParser]]

              - [[f:headingParser]]

              - [[f:paragraphParser]]

              - ... /etc/

All of these parsers live inside the [[type:OrgParser]] monadic type, which is
defined as a state monad transformer (~StateT~) with [[data:OrgParserState]], which
wraps around the inner ~Parsec Void Text~ monad, where ~Void~ is the custom error
component and ~Text~ is the type of the text being parsed. Using ~Void~ for the
custom error component means that we'll be defaulting to the default error
handling mechanism that ships with Megaparsec.

#+name: type:OrgParser
#+begin_src haskell
type OrgParser :: Type -> Type
type OrgParser = StateT OrgParserState (<<Concrete type for Megaparsec>>)
#+end_src

The [[Concrete type for Megaparsec]] feeds the concrete ~Text~ type to use as the
stream for Megaparsec. Megaparsec defines a polymorphic API and expects users to
use concrete types like this for speed optimizations.

#+name: Concrete type for Megaparsec
#+begin_src haskell
MP.ParsecT Void Text Identity
#+end_src

** ~OrgParserState~
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       6cf4d5a5-ec4a-426e-8549-0a899ad77729
:END:

The [[data:OrgParserState]] is the custom state we want to use while parsing. It's a
mutable variable which we keep updating as we make progress during parsing.

#+name: data:OrgParserState
#+begin_src haskell
type OrgParserState :: Type
data OrgParserState = OrgParserState
  { docOid :: OID, -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-docOid)
    headingOidStack :: [Maybe OID], -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-headingOidStack)
    headingLevel :: Int, -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-headingLevel)
    headingSectionNumber :: [Int], -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-headingSectionNumber)
    insideHeading :: Bool, -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-insideHeading)
    cellNames :: Set Text, -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-cellNames)
    cellNumber :: LCI, -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-cellNumber)
    pseudoEdges :: [PseudoEdge], -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-pseudoEdges)
    tanglePaths :: Set Text, -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-tanglePaths)
    insideCodeBlock :: Bool, -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-insideCodeBlock)
    anonBlockNumber :: Int, -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-anonBlockNumber)
    lineLabelNumber :: Int, -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-lineLabelNumber)
    lineLabels :: Map Text Int, -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-lineLabels)
    styleMask :: StyleMask, -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-styleMask)
    insideLinkDisplayName :: Bool, -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-insideLinkDisplayName)
    insideParagraph :: Bool, -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-insideParagraph)
    listLevels :: [Level], -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-listLevels)
    listMarkers :: [ListMarker], -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-listMarkers)
    continuationIndent :: Int, -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-continuationIndent)
    footnoteNumber :: Int, -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-footnoteNumber)
    footnoteRefs :: Map Text (Int, LCI), -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-footnoteRefs)
    footnoteDefinitions :: Map Text Prose, -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-footnoteDefinitions)
    insideFootnoteDefinition :: Maybe Text, -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-insideFootnoteDefinition)
    prevCellIsFootnote :: Bool, -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-prevCellIsFootnote)
    glossary :: Map Text LCI, -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-glossary)
    glossaryTermOffset :: Int, -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-glossaryTermOffset)
    cslPath :: Maybe FilePath, -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-cslPath)
    bibPaths :: [FilePath], -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-bibPaths)
    insideCitation :: Bool, -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-insideCitation)
    insideCitePrefix :: Bool, -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-insideCitePrefix)
    bracketBalance :: Int, -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-bracketBalance)
    citations :: [Citation], -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-citations)
    equationNumber :: Int, -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-equationNumber)
    anonEquationNumber :: Int, -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-anonEquationNumber)
    figureNumber :: Int, -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-figureNumber)
    tableNumber :: Int, -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-tableNumber)
    orgParserConf :: OrgParserConf -- (ref:data:OrgParserState-orgParserConf)
  }
  deriving stock (Eq, Show)
#+end_src

Running the ~orgDocParser~ [[(main-combinator-orgDocParser)]] requires a default set
of values for the ~OrgParserState~, which we provide with [[f:defaultOrgParserState]].

#+name: f:defaultOrgParserState
#+begin_src haskell
defaultOrgParserState :: OrgParserState
defaultOrgParserState =
  OrgParserState
    { docOid = nilOid,
      headingOidStack = [],
      headingLevel = 0,
      headingSectionNumber = [],
      insideHeading = False,
      cellNames = Set.empty,
      cellNumber = LCI 0,
      pseudoEdges = [],
      tanglePaths = Set.empty,
      insideCodeBlock = False,
      anonBlockNumber = 0,
      lineLabelNumber = 0,
      lineLabels = M.empty,
      styleMask = styleMaskFrom [],
      insideLinkDisplayName = False,
      insideParagraph = False,
      listLevels = [],
      listMarkers = [],
      continuationIndent = 0,
      footnoteNumber = 0,
      footnoteRefs = M.empty,
      footnoteDefinitions = M.empty,
      insideFootnoteDefinition = Nothing,
      prevCellIsFootnote = False,
      glossary = M.empty,
      glossaryTermOffset = 0,
      cslPath = Nothing,
      bibPaths = [],
      insideCitation = False,
      insideCitePrefix = False,
      bracketBalance = 0,
      citations = [],
      equationNumber = 0,
      anonEquationNumber = 0,
      figureNumber = 0,
      tableNumber = 0,
      orgParserConf = defaultOrgParserConf
    }
#+end_src

Now let's examine all of the various fields of [[data:OrgParserState]].

*** Fields for headings

There are a couple things to keep track of for [[id:16a68a74-d4cc-41a4-ac77-cbca77de2a31][Headings]].

  1) Headings may or may not have their own OID. Typically, OIDs for headings
     are only created when running ~org-store-link~ in Emacs, which will
     automatically create one for us when we want to refer to a named cell (you
     put your cursor over the cell and run ~org-store-link~). All named cells
     within a heading use the closest OID when going up the heading hierarchy.

  2) Headings also have a /section number/, starting with 1 and incrementing with
     subsequent headings at the same level. Each level has its own counter,
     separated with a dot. E.g., "1.2" and "2.13" are valid section numbers. We
     need to keep track of these numbers (and know how to increment them, etc),
     as we parse headings at different levels.

The fields below mostly deal with one of these two concerns.

The ~docOid~ [[(data:OrgParserState-docOid)]] is what becomes the ~oid~ [[(data:OrgDoc-oid)]] of
[[data:OrgDoc]]. If there are no headings, then this ~docOid~ is what is used as a
unique prefix to identify named cells (any cell with a ~#+name: ...~, such as a
code block) from other Org files.

The ~headingOidStack~ [[(data:OrgParserState-headingOidStack)]] is a stack of OIDs, with the
first one being the ~docOid~ and later ones being OIDs of headings encountered at
deeper levels. This is used to keep track of the current OID to use for named
cells; if we are at a deeply nested heading with its own OID, but then move out
of it, we need to know which OID to use. Using ~headingOidStack~, we just pop off
those ones we don't need any more and refer to the OID of the closest heading up
the hierarchy (ending at the ~docOid~ if needed as a fallback).

The ~headingLevel~ [[(data:OrgParserState-headingLevel)]] is the last-seen heading level we
parsed. See [[t:0004-headings-cannot-skip-level]] for how we use it. This is used
for determining how many levels to pop off the ~headingOidStack~ when we exit a
nested level. It's also used to calculate the ~headingSectionNumber~.

The ~headingSectionNumber~ [[(data:OrgParserState-headingSectionNumber)]] is the last-generated
heading section number. This is what we use to assign each new heading its
section number (by incrementing the correct part of it).

The ~insideHeading~ [[(data:OrgParserState-insideHeading)]] boolean is used to keep track of
whether we are currently parsing things from inside a heading or not. This is
used, for example, to disable parsing of citations from inside headings; i.e.,
citations are not recognized when they are placed inside headings (see
[[f:proseParser]]).

*** Fields for cells

Cell names must be unique inside a single Org file, and this is tracked with
~cellNames~ [[(data:OrgParserState-cellNames)]].

All cells, even if they are not named, are sequentially numbered in ~cellNumber~
[[(data:OrgParserState-cellNumber)]]. This is used to encode the starting vertex of each edge
in ~pseudoEdges~ [[(data:OrgParserState-pseudoEdges)]] (see [[id:3dcdfd21-5e2e-45bf-90bd-3a6a919724c5][~pseudoEdges~]]).

*** Fields for code blocks

The ~tanglePaths~ [[(data:OrgParserState-tanglePaths)]] is used in [[f:assertUniqueTanglePath]] to
check that no two blocks tangle to the same filesystem path.

The ~insideCodeBlock~ boolean [[(data:OrgParserState-insideCodeBlock)]] tracks whether we're
inside a code block; see [[f:compressedTextParser]] for how this is used.

The ~anonBlockNumber~ [[(data:OrgParserState-anonBlockNumber)]] is used to keep track of all
anonymous blocks. Anonymous blocks can link out to other named blocks, and for
such links we have to refer back to the anonymous block. As these blocks are
anonymous we have to give them an artificial name, which is the anonymous block
number (a sequential counter). See [[f:blockParser]] for details.

*** Fields for line labels

The ~lineLabelNumber~ [[(data:OrgParserState-lineLabelNumber)]] is a sequential counter for [[id:da4aa2a7-3896-4969-aa5e-5e3d842c35db][Line
labels]] which is reset at every code block.

The ~lineLabels~ map [[(data:OrgParserState-lineLabels)]] associates the label with the label
number, and this number is used to display the link in the text (source) as well as the code block (destination).

*** Fields for prose text

The ~styleMask~ [[(data:OrgParserState-styleMask)]] is discussed in [[id:b616c1e2-6b42-41cf-8e5d-c1832f95b59b][the section on parsing
styled strings]], which are one of the main building blocks of [[id:c8616126-215d-49be-8874-c2aca7d93a54][Prose]].

The ~insideLinkDisplayName~ [[(data:OrgParserState-insideLinkDisplayName)]] and ~insideParagraph~
[[(data:OrgParserState-insideParagraph)]] booleans are both used in [[f:textParser]].

*** Fields for list items

The ~listLevels~ [[(data:OrgParserState-listLevels)]] and ~listMarkers~ [[(data:OrgParserState-listMarkers)]]
are discussed in [[id:58f69993-599d-4389-ba47-3056b1bb2350][List items]].

Indentation is very important for knowing which things belong under which list
items, and the ~continuationIndent~ [[(data:OrgParserState-continuationIndent)]] is used to keep
track of how much indentation to expect inside a single (contiguous) piece of
content; see [[f:paragraphParser]] and [[f:listHeadIndentLevelParser]] for a usage
example.

*** Fields for footnotes

[[id:4127feee-755d-430e-8fef-192e95143790][Footnotes]] are numbered (~footnoteNumber~ [[(data:OrgParserState-footnoteNumber)]]), and also
have an association with cell numbers (~footnoteRefs~ [[(data:OrgParserState-footnoteRefs)]]).
The footnote definitions are kept in a single map (~footnoteDefinitions~
[[(data:OrgParserState-footnoteDefinitions)]]).

Like other booleans that tweak the behavior of the parser, footnotes also need
to do this via ~insideFootnoteDefinition~ [[(data:OrgParserState-insideFootnoteDefinition)]].

Footnote definitions must be kept in a single group of contiguous cells, and
this is checked with ~prevCellIsFootnote~ [[(data:OrgParserState-prevCellIsFootnote)]] (also see
[[f:footnoteDefinitionParser]]).

*** Fields for the glossary

[[id:73acf466-6941-4c3b-9d6d-e0843d9b0480][Description lists, aka glossary terms]] are tracked with the ~glossary~
[[(data:OrgParserState-glossary)]]. The ~glossaryTermOffset~ [[(data:OrgParserState-glossaryTermOffset)]] is
used to improve error messages (see [[t:0015-glossary-duplicate-term]]).kj

*** Fields for citations

[[id:a54a7762-a861-42b1-b2c8-2b760281052c][Citations]] require a number of fields. The ~cslPath~ [[(data:OrgParserState-cslPath)]],
~bibPaths~ [[(data:OrgParserState-bibPaths)]], and ~citations~ [[(data:OrgParserState-citations)]] fields are the same in purpose as the ones discussed at [[id:a72a7d6a-a127-4233-b67a-3800961ef766][Citations]] up above.

The ~insideCitation~ [[(data:OrgParserState-insideCitation)]], ~insideCitePrefix~
[[(data:OrgParserState-insideCitePrefix)]], and ~bracketBalance~ [[(data:OrgParserState-bracketBalance)]]
fields all deal with the lower level details of parsing these citations
correctly (being strict about what we accept).

*** Fields for miscellaneous sequential counters

Mathematical equations, figures, and tables all need to get numbered. These are
handled by ~equationNumber~ [[(data:OrgParserState-equationNumber)]], ~anonEquationNumber~
[[(data:OrgParserState-anonEquationNumber)]], ~figureNumber~ [[(data:OrgParserState-figureNumber)]], and
~tableNumber~ [[(data:OrgParserState-tableNumber)]].

*** Fields for Lilac extensions

[[id:84edd33a-a146-4a46-ac0a-cd1c3d0926d3][Lilac extensions]] can alter the behavior of the parser. These are encapsulated in
the [[data:OrgParserConf]], which we capture in ~orgParserConf~
[[(data:OrgParserState-orgParserConf)]].

* =Lilac.Parse= module
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       5ce50c9a-5413-48d9-8bba-e465a4eb527c
:END:

The parsing mechanism is in the ~Lilac.Parse~ module. We discuss each parser and
interleave some tests for the smaller parsers. If you are curious about the
overall testing framework itself (where and how these interleaved tests are
executed), take a look at the [[id:ebf0d269-79d3-408a-a6e7-baf720d73837][Testing framework]].

* ~orgDocParser~
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       0aa99372-14d1-4023-8db9-85b9078bc498
:END:

At a high level, the [[f:orgDocParser]] delegates most of the work to the
[[f:cellParser]], which in turn delegates work again to the individual parsers that
know how to parse different cells. We have to filter out cells that look like
~CListItem []~ with ~meaningfulCell~ [[(drop-meaningless-cells)]] because they are a
by-product of parsing [[id:58f69993-599d-4389-ba47-3056b1bb2350][List items]] (this can happen when [[f:listItemsEndParser]]
detects maximum indentation that matches the most-deeply-nested item (in which
case no list items end)).

#+name: f:orgDocParser
#+begin_src haskell
orgDocParser :: OrgParser OrgDoc
orgDocParser = do
  _ <- optional skipNewlinesAndComments -- (ref:first-newlines-comments-skip)
  (docOid, cslPath, bibPaths, meta) <- docMetaParser -- (ref:doc-metadata-parsing)
  _ <- lilacExtensionsParser
  cells <- many cellParser
  MP.eof
  footnoteRefs <- reconcileFootnotes
  citations <- collectCitations
  pState <- get
  resetState
  let meaningfulCell :: Cell -> Bool
      meaningfulCell = \case
        CListItem [] -> False
        CHeading {}
        CListItem {}
        CParagraph {}
        CGlossaryTerm {}
        CBlock {}
        CMathFragment {}
        CFigure {}
        CTable {}
        CFootnote {}
        CCommand {} -> True
  let meaningfulCells = filter meaningfulCell cells -- (ref:drop-meaningless-cells)
  when (pState.cellNumber.unLCI /= length meaningfulCells)
    $ fail
      ( "mismatch: cellNumber: "
          <> show pState.cellNumber
          <> " len meaningfulCells: "
          <> (show $ length meaningfulCells)
      )
  pure
    $ OrgDoc
      { oid = docOid,
        cells = meaningfulCells,
        pseudoEdges = pState.pseudoEdges,
        footnoteRefs = footnoteRefs,
        glossary = pState.glossary,
        lineLabels = pState.lineLabels,
        cslPath = cslPath,
        bibPaths = bibPaths,
        citations = citations,
        meta = deleteRedundantKeys meta ["cite_export"] -- (ref:deleteRedundantKeys)
      }
#+end_src

We now look at some of the less obvious parts (other than [[f:cellParser]] for
[[id:ed81c307-9172-4436-8672-583ea8ecb1f9][Cells]]) first:

  - *Initial leading newlines and comments*. The [[f:orgDocParser]] first starts by
    skipping any leading whitespace/comments [[(first-newlines-comments-skip)]]. We
    have to do this first because all of the other parsers we use will follow
    the pattern of parsing the thing we want, followed by skipping over as much
    trailing whitespace (but not leading indentation) as possible. In other
    words, none of the parsers are able to deal with leading
    whitespace/comments, so we have to deal with it here separately as the very
    first thing we check for.

  - *Document metadata*. We need to parse any metadata associated with the
    document [[(doc-metadata-parsing)]], such as the document ID, title and author.
    See [[f:docMetaParser]] for details.

  - *Reconciliation of some data structures*. Once we've finished parsing the
    entire document, we can then make assertions such as "all footnote
    references must have a corresponding definition" or "all referenced line
    labels must exist in code blocks". We call this process /reconciliation/ and
    this is done for footnotes and line labels.

  - *Resetting of some stateful fields*. Some of our unit tests check for the
    state of the [[data:OrgParserState]] with the [[f:shouldParseWithState]] helper;
    more on this at

** Whitespace and comments
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       fbca0c4e-c568-4c12-b89c-7cf334b8d50f
:END:

Before writing our first parsers, we have to consider the problem of whitespace
and comments.

Any syntax that is expected to be used by humans have some designation for
whitespace to separate meaningful tokens from each other. One way to deal with
(necessary) whitespace is to bake in whitespace parsers into each token we want
to parse. This gets cumbersome so we want to instead be able to teach parsers to
be able to skip over trailing whitespace. This way, these parsers will all know
how to deal with whitespace in a uniform way. For example, this is what we do in
[[f:cellParser]] where we wrap all of the major ~Cell~ parsers with a ~lexeme~.

A slightly different problem is how to deal with comments. In a sense they are
just like whitespace because they hold no meaningful information on their own.
Luckily, ~Text.Megaparsec.Char.Lexer~ provides a ~space~ helper which can create a
parser that skips over whitespace as well as comments. This is what we use below
to define [[f:skipNewlinesAndComments]]. See [[https://markkarpov.com/tutorial/megaparsec.html#lexing][the Megaparsec tutorial]] for more
information.

#+name: f:skipNewlinesAndComments
#+begin_src haskell
skipNewlinesAndComments :: OrgParser ()
skipNewlinesAndComments =
  L.space whiteSpaceParser singleLineCommentParser blockCommentParser -- (ref:L.space-arguments)
  where
    whiteSpaceParser :: OrgParser ()
    whiteSpaceParser = do
      _ <- MP.takeWhile1P (Just "newline") (== '\n') -- (ref:whiteSpaceParser-newline-only)
      pState <- get
      _ <-
        MP.optional
          . MP.label "indentation for next thing"
          $ MP.count pState.continuationIndent (char ' ')
      pure ()
    singleLineCommentParser :: OrgParser ()
    singleLineCommentParser = L.skipLineComment "# " -- (ref:singleLineCommentParser)
    blockCommentParser :: OrgParser ()
    blockCommentParser =
      L.skipBlockComment
        "#+begin_comment\n"
        "#+end_comment\n" -- (ref:blockCommentParser)
#+end_src

The three arguments to ~L.space~ [[(L.space-arguments)]] (where ~L~ stands for
~Text.Megaparsec.Char.Lexer~) are the parsers for whitespace, single-line
comments, and block comments. ~L.space~ will try to repeatedly apply the three
given parsers; it only stops parsing when faced with input where all three
parsers fail.

We only parse newline characters in ~whiteSpaceParser~
[[(whiteSpaceParser-newline-only)]]. We don't parse spaces. This is because we do
not want to skip over any /indentation/ --- that is, space characters immediately
following a newline. Such indentation could be an error (for example, for
headings or paragraphs), or a necessary part of a cell (such as ~CListItem~ cells
in [[f:listItemsEndParser]]), and so we leave that determination up to other
parsers.

It's important that we use ~takeWhile1P~ for ~whiteSpaceParser~ (where the ~1~ denotes
that it requires at least 1 newline character) because otherwise if it is
allowed to accept 0 newlines (as with ~takeWhileP~), it may loop forever upon
seeing non-newline input.

Single-line comments use ~"# "~ [[(singleLineCommentParser)]], whereas multi-line
comments expect an opening and closing delimiter [[(blockCommentParser)]]. We
include explicit newlines in ~blockCommentParser~ because we want to ensure that
block comment markers are always on their own line (e.g., ~#+end_comment~ is
valid, but ~#+end_comment foo~ is not).

~Text.Megaparsec.Char.Lexer~ also defines a ~L.skipBlockCommentNested~ helper, which
is able to understand if the ending marker of a block (~#+end_comment\n~ for us)
should be ignored because it's nested and instead skip past it to find the true
(non-nested) ending marker. That's a little too complicated, so we use the
non-nested ~L.skipBlockComment~ to keep the syntax simple.

Lastly we have [[f:lexeme]], which wraps around any other parser to make it consume
trailing newlines /and comments/. This is used in [[f:cellParser]]. We just reuse
[[f:skipNewlinesAndComments]] as the main workhorse here.

#+name: f:lexeme
#+begin_src haskell
lexeme :: OrgParser a -> OrgParser a
lexeme = L.lexeme skipNewlinesAndComments
#+end_src

** ~docMetaParser~
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       39beba98-d4c3-4c6d-99f3-3848575a8300
:END:

An Org document's metadata is the first meaningful thing that could be parsed.
Metadata can be created with a ~:PROPERTIES:~ drawer, or with a line that looks
like ~#+key: value~ where ~key~ is a string and ~value~ is a series of tokens (it can
have multiple words in it). The key and value syntax is trickier, so we look at
that first with [[f:docMetaEntryParser]] below.

#+name: f:docMetaEntryParser
#+begin_src haskell
docMetaEntryParser :: OrgParser (Text, Text)
docMetaEntryParser = do
  avoidParsingBlockMetadata -- (ref:avoidParsingBlockMetadata)
  avoidParsingLilacExtensions -- (ref:avoidParsingLilacExtensions)
  _ <- MP.notFollowedBy $ string "#+__" -- (ref:avoid-double-underscores)
  _ <- string "#+"
  key <- MP.takeWhile1P (Just "(doc meta) keyword letter") isKeywordLetter
  _ <- MP.label "(doc meta) separator" $ string ": "
  _ <- many $ char ' '
  value <- MP.takeWhile1P (Just "(doc meta) value letter") (\c -> c /= '\n')
  pure (key, value)
  where
    isKeywordLetter c = or $ map ($ c) [isAsciiLower, isAsciiUpper, (== '_')]
    avoidParsingBlockMetadata :: OrgParser ()
    avoidParsingBlockMetadata =
      mapM_
        MP.notFollowedBy
        $ map
          string
          [ "#+begin_",
            "#+name: ",
            "#+header: ",
            "#+print_bibliography:",
            "#+print_glossary:"
          ]
    avoidParsingLilacExtensions :: OrgParser ()
    avoidParsingLilacExtensions = MP.notFollowedBy $ string "#+lilac_"
#+end_src

We have to tiptoe around code blocks by checking that it is not looking at
block-related metadata syntax with ~avoidParsingBlockMetadata~
[[(avoidParsingBlockMetadata)]]. This is important because otherwise if an Org
document begins with a [[id:cae993e5-02bd-4f89-a6d3-20b41adaff8f][code block]] as the first thing (before [[id:16a68a74-d4cc-41a4-ac77-cbca77de2a31][headings]] or
anything else), we may consume that as part of ~docMetaParser~ and fail because we
think we are expecting ~: ~ followed by a value when we encounter ~#+begin_src
...~.

Similarly, we also do not parse any [[id:84edd33a-a146-4a46-ac0a-cd1c3d0926d3][Lilac extensions]]
[[(avoidParsingLilacExtensions)]] because they can only be parsed by the
[[f:lilacExtensionsParser]].

We avoid parsing keys with a double underscore [[(avoid-double-underscores)]],
because that's reserved for internal use by Lilac. Having the restriction at
[[(avoid-double-underscores)]] allows the failure condition at
[[(fail-if-double-underscore)]] in [[f:metaEntryParser]] to trigger.

The [[f:docMetaParser]] first checks for the top-level ~:PROPERTIES:~ drawer with
[[f:propertiesDrawerParser]]. Then it just parses [[f:docMetaEntryParser]] multiple
times, while consuming any trailing whitespace or comments with [[f:lexeme]].

#+name: f:docMetaParser
#+begin_src haskell
docMetaParser :: OrgParser (OID, Maybe FilePath, [FilePath], Map Text Text)
docMetaParser = do
  topLevelDrawerProps <- propertiesDrawerParser
  _ <- optional skipNewlinesAndComments
  metaKVsList <- many $ lexeme docMetaEntryParser
  let (bibPathsText, remainingKVs) = collectValsFor "bibliography" metaKVsList
      metaKVs = fromList remainingKVs
      bibPaths = map T.unpack bibPathsText
  let conflictingKeys = keys $ M.intersection topLevelDrawerProps metaKVs
  when (not $ null conflictingKeys)
    $ fail
    $ "conflicting keys in the top-level PROPERTIES drawer "
    <> "and document metadata key/values: "
    <> (show conflictingKeys)
  case lookup "ID" topLevelDrawerProps of
    Nothing -> fail $ "required document ID property not found"
    Just oidText -> do
      oid <- validateOidText oidText -- (ref:validateOidText oidText)
      cslPath <- case lookup "cite_export" metaKVs of
        Just maybeCslPath -> validateCslPath maybeCslPath
        Nothing -> pure Nothing
      modify $ \s ->
        s
          { OrgParserState.bibPaths = bibPaths,
            OrgParserState.cslPath = cslPath,
            docOid = oid
          }
      pure (oid, cslPath, bibPaths, topLevelDrawerProps <> metaKVs)
#+end_src

OID validation in [[f:validateOidText]] [[(validateOidText oidText)]] delegates to the
[[id:5908cefc-0e82-41a5-84ac-1c8c826f5e62::f:fromText][f:fromText]] helper from ~Lilac.Types~.

#+name: f:validateOidText
#+begin_src haskell
validateOidText :: (MonadFail m) => Text -> m OID
validateOidText oidText = case Lilac.Types.fromText oidText of
  Nothing -> fail $ show oidText <> " is not a valid OID"
  Just oid -> pure oid
#+end_src

Note that using the properties drawer is the main way in which the ~ID~ field of a
document is created, which is then used by Orgmode (see
~org-id-link-to-org-use-id~, ~org-id-link-consider-parent-id~, and
~org-id-locations-file~). Using ID's for things (instead of the file name) allows
us to create links that are more resilient against file name changes (or
directory changes). See [[id:ca12051e-cd17-4d8d-996a-767695441d48][Drawers]] for more discussion about properties drawers and
[[ti:0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer]].

The special key ~#+cite_export: ...~ is parsed separately as the ~cslPath~, using
the [[f:validateCslPath]] helper. This is needed for supporting [[id:a54a7762-a861-42b1-b2c8-2b760281052c][Citations]].

*** Lilac extensions
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       84edd33a-a146-4a46-ac0a-cd1c3d0926d3
:END:

Lilac supports extensions to alter the behavior of the parser for the
document being parsed. These extensions are all prefixed by ~lilac_~ and are set
like other document metadata; they must come *after* the other (typical) Org metadata.

Below is a list of supported extensions:

  - ~lilac_maxHeadingLevel~ :: [[f:maxHeadingLevelParser]] This takes an integer as a
    value. Using ~-1~ means no headings are allowed at all. Using ~0~ means headings
    can have as many asterisks as desired. Using a positive integer means
    headings may only have that many (or less) asterisks; otherwise Lilac will
    fail the parse.

  - ~lilac_maxListLevel~ :: [[f:maxListLevelParser]] This has the same semantics as
    ~lilac_maxHeadingLevel~ but is for list items, not headings.

These extensions are encoded inside the [[data:OrgParserConf]].

#+name: data:OrgParserConf
#+begin_src haskell
type OrgParserConf :: Type
data OrgParserConf = OrgParserConf
  { maxHeadingLevel :: Int,
    maxListLevel :: Int
  }
  deriving stock (Eq, Show)
#+end_src

The default preferences are saved in [[f:defaultOrgParserConf]].

#+name: f:defaultOrgParserConf
#+begin_src haskell
defaultOrgParserConf :: OrgParserConf
defaultOrgParserConf =
  OrgParserConf
    { maxHeadingLevel = 3,
      maxListLevel = 3
    }
#+end_src

If you want to deviate from the defaults, you have to specify these in your
top-level document metadata, for [[f:lilacExtensionsParser]] to pick up.

#+name: f:lilacExtensionsParser
#+begin_src haskell
lilacExtensionsParser :: OrgParser ()
lilacExtensionsParser = do
  void
    . many
    $ MP.choice
      [ maxHeadingLevelParser,
        maxListLevelParser
      ]
#+end_src

The [[f:maxHeadingLevelParser]] resembles the [[f:docMetaEntryParser]], but the main
difference is that it does not return anything (its type is ~OrgParser ()~).
Instead it just modifies the ~pState~.

#+name: f:maxHeadingLevelParser
#+begin_src haskell
maxHeadingLevelParser :: OrgParser ()
maxHeadingLevelParser = do
  pState <- get
  _ <- string $ "#+lilac_maxHeadingLevel: "
  _ <- many $ char ' '
  val <- signedIntParser
  let opc = pState.orgParserConf {maxHeadingLevel = val}
  put pState {orgParserConf = opc}
#+end_src

The [[f:signedIntParser]] knows how to parse a signed integer. The
~parserBetweenSignAndNumber~ is the parser for parsing the area between the sign
(~-~) and the number. Here we define it as ~pure ()~ meaning that nothing is
allowed. The usual use case here is to use a whitespace parser, so as to allow a
space between the sign and number, but we don't do this here for simplicity.

#+name: f:signedIntParser
#+begin_src haskell
signedIntParser :: OrgParser Int
signedIntParser = L.signed parserBetweenSignAndNumber number
  where
    number :: OrgParser Int
    number = L.lexeme skipNewlinesAndComments $ L.decimal
    parserBetweenSignAndNumber :: OrgParser ()
    parserBetweenSignAndNumber = pure ()
#+end_src

[[f:maxListLevelParser]] closely resembles [[f:maxHeadingLevelParser]], and similarly
updates the ~orgParserConf~ field inside [[f:defaultOrgParserState]].

#+name: f:maxListLevelParser
#+begin_src haskell
maxListLevelParser :: OrgParser ()
maxListLevelParser = do
  pState <- get
  _ <- string $ "#+lilac_maxListLevel: "
  _ <- many $ char ' '
  val <- signedIntParser
  let opc = pState.orgParserConf {maxListLevel = val}
  put pState {orgParserConf = opc}
#+end_src

** Resetting of state
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       6a181f3b-b12e-451f-b195-5775b05aab65
:END:

We call [[f:resetState]] in [[f:orgDocParser]] to clear some state from
[[data:OrgParserState]], because otherwise it gets too polluted during testing.
Also, it's not like we're throwing away information because everything we need
is still captured elsewhere (just check the fields that have the same name in
both [[data:OrgParserState]] and [[data:OrgDoc]]).

#+name: f:resetState
#+begin_src haskell
resetState :: OrgParser ()
resetState =
  modify $ \s ->
    s
      { OrgParserState.bibPaths = [],
        OrgParserState.citations = [],
        OrgParserState.cslPath = Nothing,
        OrgParserState.cellNumber = LCI 0,
        OrgParserState.pseudoEdges = []
      }
#+end_src

In [[f:resetState]] we cannot simply refer to the field alone in the record update,
like this

#+begin_src haskell
  modify (\s -> s {citations = []})
#+end_src

because it will result in an [[https://errors.haskell.org/messages/GHC-02256/]["Ambiguous record update" error]], because both
[[data:OrgDoc]] and [[data:OrgParserState]] have a field named ~citations~. So we work
around this by importing the latter through ~import Lilac.Types as OrgParserState~
in [[module:Lilac.Parse]]. See [[https://discourse.haskell.org/t/what-are-you-supposed-to-do-about-wambiguous-fields/9545][this discussion from 2024]] for more information.

In a similar vein to [[f:resetState]], [[f:deleteRedundantKeys]] [[(deleteRedundantKeys)]]
removes certain entries from the given map data structure. In the case of
[[f:orgDocParser]], it removes keys that won't be used because the information in
those keys have already been parsed and saved elsewhere.

#+name: f:deleteRedundantKeys
#+begin_src haskell
deleteRedundantKeys :: (Ord k) => Map k v -> [k] -> Map k v
deleteRedundantKeys = foldl' (flip M.delete)
#+end_src

** Cells
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       ed81c307-9172-4436-8672-583ea8ecb1f9
:END:

The /cell/ is the main building block of an Org document --- it is roughly any
region of text surrounded by blank lines.

#+name: data:Cell
#+begin_src haskell
type Cell :: Type
data Cell
  = CHeading Heading
  | CListItem [ListMarker]
  | CParagraph Prose
  | CBlock Block
  | CMathFragment MathFragment
  | CFigure Figure
  | CTable Table
  | CFootnote Footnote
  | CGlossaryTerm GlossaryTerm
  | CCommand Command
  deriving stock (Eq, Ord, Generic, Show)
  deriving anyclass (FromJSON, ToJSON)
#+end_src

All of the different cell types describe some sort of content, except for one,
~CListItem~. The ~CListItem~ is purely structural because it only describes itself
and relies on /other cells/ for displaying any useful information. See
[[id:58f69993-599d-4389-ba47-3056b1bb2350][List items]] to see how this is used.

The [[data:Command]] encodes information about /generated/ content, such as [[id:e24de101-bf1d-418b-a18f-b1a98fea9696][Printing
bibliographies]].

#+name: data:Command
#+begin_src haskell
type Command :: Type
data Command
  = PrintBibliography
  | PrintGlossary Bool
  deriving stock (Eq, Ord, Generic, Show)
  deriving anyclass (FromJSON, ToJSON)
#+end_src

The [[f:cellParser]] is responsible for parsing these different recognized values of
[[data:Cell]].

#+name: f:cellParser
#+begin_src haskell
cellParser :: OrgParser Cell
cellParser = do
  cell <-
    lexeme
      $ MP.choice
        [ toggleFootnote False $ listItemStartParser,
          toggleFootnote False $ listItemsEndParser,
          toggleFootnote False $ resetIndent headingParser,
          toggleFootnote False $ resetIndent blockParser,
          toggleFootnote False $ resetIndent figureParser,
          toggleFootnote False $ resetIndent tableParser,
          toggleFootnote False $ resetIndent mathFragmentStandaloneParser,
          toggleFootnote True $ resetIndent footnoteDefinitionParser,
          toggleFootnote False $ resetIndent bibliographyLocationParser,
          toggleFootnote False $ resetIndent glossaryLocationParser,
          toggleFootnote False $ resetIndent paragraphParser,
          detectInvalidIndentation
        ]
  modify (\s -> s {bracketBalance = 0}) -- (ref:f:cellParser-bracketBalance-reset)
  when (cell /= CListItem [])
    $ modify (\s -> s {cellNumber = LCI $ s.cellNumber.unLCI + 1})
  pure cell
  where
    resetIndent :: OrgParser a -> OrgParser a -- (ref:f:cellParser-resetIndent)
    resetIndent p = do
      a <- p
      modify (\s -> s {continuationIndent = 0})
      pure a
    toggleFootnote :: Bool -> OrgParser a -> OrgParser a
    toggleFootnote b p = do
      a <- p
      modify (\s -> s {prevCellIsFootnote = b})
      pure a
#+end_src

The ~resetIndent~ wrapper [[(f:cellParser-resetIndent)]] resets the ~continuationIndent~
attribute [[(data:OrgParserState-continuationIndent)]] in the [[data:OrgParserState]] back to 0;
this information is useful for parsers that need to understand indentation
precisely. See [[f:listItemsEndParser]] for how it's used by that function.

The [[f:listItemStartParser]] and [[f:listItemsEndParser]] are the only parsers that can
consume leading indentation! The [[f:paragraphParser]] can consume leading
indentation, but /only/ on lines except the first one (so that it can ensure that
paragraphs that are part of a list item can have all of its lines line up
together). For example, the following paragraph is inside a list item

#+begin_src org
  - Hello
    world.
#+end_src

and will be parsed as a string ~Hello world~ and not as ~Hello    world~ (the
leading indent for ~world~ will be ignored).

Any other indentation should be treated as being invalid. To enforce this, we
put in a [[f:detectInvalidIndentation]] parser which technically isn't a parser but
which will throw an error if it is able to successfully parse indentation. The
idea is that if none of the other parsers in the list of parsers in [[f:cellParser]]
are able to consume indentation, then this will allow ~detectInvalidIndentation~
to see such indentation and fail the overall parser.

#+name: f:detectInvalidIndentation
#+begin_src haskell
detectInvalidIndentation :: OrgParser Cell
detectInvalidIndentation = do
  _ <- MP.hidden $ onlySpaces
  void
    $ MP.fancyFailure -- (ref:f:detectInvalidIndentation-MP.fancyFailure)
    . Set.singleton
    . MP.ErrorFail
    $ "invalid indentation" -- (ref:f:detectInvalidIndentation-errorMsg)
  pure $ CListItem [] -- (ref:f:detectInvalidIndentation-CListItem)
#+end_src

#+name: f:onlySpaces
#+begin_src haskell
onlySpaces :: OrgParser Text
onlySpaces = MP.takeWhile1P (Just "space") (== ' ') -- (ref:f:detectInvalidIndentation-MP.takeWhile1P)
#+end_src

We signal the error with ~invalid ...~ [[(f:detectInvalidIndentation-errorMsg)]] so as
to not shadow the typical ~unexpected ...~ language from Megaparsec's usual parse
failures. Note also that the ~pure $ CListItem []~
[[(f:detectInvalidIndentation-CListItem)]] is only there to satisfy the type checker
--- we will always fail if the ~takeWhile1P~
[[(f:detectInvalidIndentation-MP.takeWhile1P)]] succeeds (the ~MP.fancyFailure~
[[(f:detectInvalidIndentation-MP.fancyFailure)]] will not be executed if ~takeWhile1P~
fails).

The ~bracketBalance~ is set to 0 [[(f:cellParser-bracketBalance-reset)]] in
[[f:cellParser]] after a cell is parsed, because we're not interested in cross-cell
issues about unbalanced brackets. See [[f:failIfUnbalancedBrackets]].

The rest of the top-level headings this document is mainly concerned with
describing all of the different building blocks used for constructing cells.

** Tests
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       765aab71-b548-4aef-8895-0e3c75198041
:END:

These tests check for mostly empty documents, and also those with some metadata.

We should be able to parse empty input.

#+name: t:empty-input
#+begin_src haskell
it "empty input" $ do
  MP.runParser
    (runStateT L.orgDocParser defaultOrgParserState)
    "empty input"
    nilOidOrgDocPrefix
    `shouldParse` (L.defaultOrgDoc nilOid [], defaultOrgParserState)
#+end_src

The [[f:defaultOrgDoc]] is just a default (mostly blank) ~OrgDoc~. It's useful for
testing.

#+name: f:defaultOrgDoc
#+begin_src haskell
defaultOrgDoc :: OID -> [(Text, Text)] -> OrgDoc
defaultOrgDoc oid metas =
  OrgDoc
    { oid = oid,
      cells = [],
      pseudoEdges = [],
      footnoteRefs = M.empty,
      glossary = M.empty,
      lineLabels = M.empty,
      cslPath = Nothing,
      bibPaths = [],
      citations = [],
      meta = fromList metas'
    }
  where
    metas' =
      if oid == nilOid
        then ("ID", "00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000") : metas
        else metas
#+end_src

We should also be able to parse blank documents (not empty, but only has
whitespace).

#+name: ti:0001-blankline
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0001-blankline
#+begin_src org


#+end_src

We have to put in two newlines above because during parsing we will lose one
newline (it is used to parse the ~"\n#+end_src"~ string; see [[f:someBTokensParser]]).
This leaves us with just one newline in
[[file:test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0001-blankline]].

#+name: t:0001-blankline
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0001-blankline"
  (L.defaultOrgDoc nilOid [])
#+end_src

Aside: [[ti:0001-blankline]] in HTML looks like there is no content (this is
intentional), because [[f:zeroBTokensParser]] parses it the same way it parses a
block with zero bytes in it (see [[ti:0007-blocks-empty]]).

Parsing comments should also result in a blank document.

#+name: ti:0001-comments-only
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0001-comments-only
#+begin_src org
# This is a single-line comment.

,#+begin_comment
This is inside
a block comment.
,#+end_comment
#+end_src

#+name: t:0001-comments-only
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0001-comments-only"
  (L.defaultOrgDoc nilOid [])
#+end_src

At the other end of the spectrum, Lilac should be able to parse itself.

#+name: t:self-parse
#+begin_src haskell
before (LU.readFrom "" "index.org") $ do
  it "self-parse" $ \input -> do
    MP.runParser
      (runStateT L.orgDocParser defaultOrgParserState)
      "index.org"
      `shouldSucceedOn` input
#+end_src

*** Document metadata

Below are some unit test cases for parsing metadata.

#+name: ti:0002-doc-metadata:basic
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0002-doc-metadata
#+begin_src org
,#+title: An Org Document
,#+author: A. U. Thor
,#+property: header-args+ :noweb no-export
,#+auto_tangle: t
#+end_src

#+name: t:0002-doc-metadata:basic
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0002-doc-metadata"
  $ L.defaultOrgDoc
    nilOid
    [ ("author", "A. U. Thor"),
      ("auto_tangle", "t"),
      ("property", "header-args+ :noweb no-export"),
      ("title", "An Org Document")
    ]
#+end_src

Note that we currently don't do any intelligent processing of the metadata
fields. For example, the value of the ~property~ key above is a mess because it
itself contains a key and value (~:noweb~ and ~no-export~).

Below is the same as the test above, but with some whitespace and comments
sprinkled in between.

#+name: ti:0003-doc-metadata-with-comments
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0003-doc-metadata-with-comments
#+begin_src org
,#+title: An Org Document
# This is a comment line.
,#+author: A. U. Thor
# This is another comment line.


# Note the whitespace above this line.
,#+property: header-args+ :noweb no-export
# Below is a block comment.
,#+begin_comment
Yet another comment.
,#+end_comment
,#+auto_tangle: t

# Last comment followed by some trailing newlines.





#+end_src

#+name: t:0003-doc-metadata-with-comments
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0003-doc-metadata-with-comments"
  $ L.defaultOrgDoc
    nilOid
    [ ("author", "A. U. Thor"),
      ("auto_tangle", "t"),
      ("property", "header-args+ :noweb no-export"),
      ("title", "An Org Document")
    ]
#+end_src

Indentation is invalid for document metadata. Even single-line comments may not
be indented.

#+name: ti:0008-indentation-comment
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0008-indentation-comment
#+begin_src org
 # invalid comment (it's indented)
#+end_src

#+name: t:0008-indentation-comment
#+begin_src haskell
shouldFailWithMessage
  "0008-indentation-comment"
  "invalid indentation"
  1
#+end_src

Metadata values can have leading whitespace. We ignore such whitespace.

#+name: ti:0002-doc-metadata-whitespace-before-value
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0002-doc-metadata-whitespace-before-value
#+begin_src org
,#+title:             Parse me without leading whitespace
#+end_src

#+name: t:0002-doc-metadata-whitespace-before-value
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0002-doc-metadata-whitespace-before-value"
  $ L.defaultOrgDoc
    nilOid
    [ ("title", "Parse me without leading whitespace")
    ]
#+end_src

Metadata values are required (there must be some non-whitespace text for it).

#+name: ti:0002-doc-metadata-missing-value
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0002-doc-metadata-missing-value
#+begin_src org
,#+title:
#+end_src

#+name: t:0002-doc-metadata-missing-value
#+begin_src haskell
shouldFailWithError
  "0002-doc-metadata-missing-value"
  ( utoks ":\n"
      <> elabel "(doc meta) separator"
      <> elabel "(doc meta) keyword letter"
  )
  7
#+end_src

Metadata can also come from top-level properties drawers.

#+name: ti:0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer
#+begin_src org
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       7af1aa3e-41ca-4810-a001-2fdf10768f2f
:END:
#+end_src

#+name: t:0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWithState
  "0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer"
  ( L.defaultOrgDoc
      (fromWords 0x7af1aa3e41ca4810 0xa0012fdf10768f2f)
      [ ("ID", "7af1aa3e-41ca-4810-a001-2fdf10768f2f")
      ]
  )
  defaultOrgParserState
    { docOid = fromWords 0x7af1aa3e41ca4810 0xa0012fdf10768f2f
    }
#+end_src

If the ~:ID:~ is missing, we assign a nil (all zero bits) OID. We reuse the same
test case file ([[ti:0001-blankline]]) from [[t:0001-blankline]].

#+name: t:0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer-empty
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWithState
  "0001-blankline"
  (L.defaultOrgDoc nilOid [])
  defaultOrgParserState
#+end_src

Metadata from the top-level properties drawer and also the key/value metadata
lines get combined together into a single map.

#+name: ti:0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer-combine
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer-combine
#+begin_src org
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       7af1aa3e-41ca-4810-a001-2fdf10768f2f
:END:
,#+title: An Org Document
,#+author: A. U. Thor
,#+property: header-args+ :noweb no-export
#+end_src

#+name: t:0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer-combine
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWithState
  "0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer-combine"
  ( L.defaultOrgDoc
      (fromWords 0x7af1aa3e41ca4810 0xa0012fdf10768f2f)
      [ ("ID", "7af1aa3e-41ca-4810-a001-2fdf10768f2f"),
        ("author", "A. U. Thor"),
        ("property", "header-args+ :noweb no-export"),
        ("title", "An Org Document")
      ]
  )
  defaultOrgParserState
    { docOid = fromWords 0x7af1aa3e41ca4810 0xa0012fdf10768f2f
    }
#+end_src

If there is a clash, error out instead of silently choosing precedence.

#+name: ti:0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer-clash
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer-clash
#+begin_src org
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000
:foo:      bar
:END:
,#+title: An Org Document
,#+author: A. U. Thor
,#+property: header-args+ :noweb no-export
,#+foo: xyz
#+end_src

#+name: t:0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer-clash
#+begin_src haskell
before
  ( LU.readFrom
      "test/Lilac/ParseSpec/"
      "0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer-clash"
  )
  $ do
    it "0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer-clash" $ \input -> do
      MP.runParser
        (runStateT L.orgDocParser defaultOrgParserState)
        "0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer-clash"
        input
        `shouldFailWith` ( errFancy 181
                             . fancy
                             $ MP.ErrorFail
                               ( "conflicting keys in the top-level PROPERTIES"
                                   <> " drawer and document metadata"
                                   <> " key/values: [\"foo\"]"
                               )
                         )
#+end_src

* Styled text
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       b616c1e2-6b42-41cf-8e5d-c1832f95b59b
:END:

A [[data:StyledText]] is just some text that has one or more styles attached to it.
Here are some examples: *bold*, /italic/, _underline_, =verbatim=, and ~code~.

#+name: data:StyledText
#+begin_src haskell
type StyledText :: Type
data StyledText = StyledText
  { body :: Text,
    style :: StyleMask
  }
  deriving stock (Eq, Generic, Ord, Show)
  deriving anyclass (FromJSON, ToJSON)
#+end_src

The [[newtype:StyleMask]] is a low-level bit mask that can store multiple boolean
values of each of the supported [[data:Style]] bits. If the string has no style,
then it's not stylized at all and the mask will be 0 (no bits set). See
[[id:87cd7d62-1f5c-4442-87ff-e1f2a68a8e56][=StyleMask= details]] for a discussion of how this data structure is used and
transformed into a list of [[data:Style]] values. For now, we can just pretend that
the ~StyleMask~ is like a list of [[data:Style]] values.

In order to save some keystrokes (especially in test cases), we use a couple
functions, [[f:stext]] and [[f:ptext]].

#+name: f:stext
#+begin_src haskell
stext :: Text -> [Style] -> StyledText
stext body styles = StyledText {body = body, style = styleMaskFrom styles}
#+end_src

#+name: f:ptext
#+begin_src haskell
ptext :: Text -> [Style] -> PToken
ptext body styles = PTokenStyledText $ stext body styles
#+end_src

The [[data:Style]] type is straightforward enough. For now we don't support all
possible styles supported by Orgmode (such as strike-through text), for both
simplicity but also prescriptive reasons. For example, strike-through text
should never be needed during exposition.

#+name: data:Style
#+begin_src haskell
type Style :: Type
data Style
  = Bold
  | Italic
  | Underline
  | Verbatim
  | Code
  | Smallcaps
  | Subscript
  | Superscript
  deriving stock (Enum, Eq, Generic, Ord, Show)
  deriving anyclass (FromJSON, ToJSON)
#+end_src

The [[f:styleMarkers]] keeps track of the characters that activate and deactivate
each style. It has the syntactic characters we use to /mark/ text with the a
supported /style/.

We don't actually allow users to use the ~Smallcaps~, ~Subscript~, or ~Superscript~
styles themselves (this is why they're missing from [[f:styleMarkers]]); these are
used inside [[id:7874b9c5-ceac-4820-8804-7732e29f5c60::f:cslJsonTextToProse][f:cslJsonTextToProse]] and [[id:d6abea81-d620-4ede-9c2c-68f7ea07f847::f:weaveStyledText][f:weaveStyledText]] to preserve these styles
that ~CslJson~ might generate for citation processing.

#+name: f:styleMarkers
#+begin_src haskell
styleMarkers :: Map Char Style
styleMarkers =
  fromList
    [ ('*', Bold),
      ('/', Italic),
      ('_', Underline),
      ('=', Verbatim),
      ('~', Code)
    ]
#+end_src

Characters in the [[f:styleMarkers]] can be escaped with a backslash. For example,
the strings ~1\*2~ and ~a\_b~ are interpreted as ~1*2~ and ~a_b~, because they act to
escape the style markers ~*~ and ~_~, respectively. If you want to write a literal
backslash, you must use ~\\~. This use of the backslash as an escape character
deviates from Orgmode.

This rule also applies for slashes, such as ~1\/2~ in
[[ti:0005-styled-strings-escape-marker]]. However, for slashes there is a simpler
(and arguably more intuitive syntax) where slashes inside a word are not treated
as style markers, so they don't need to be escaped. See [[f:slashSeparatedWords]]
and [[ti:0005-styled-strings-slash-separated-words]].

#+name: ti:0005-styled-strings-escape-marker
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0005-styled-strings-escape-marker
#+begin_src org
/foo and\/or 1\/2 bar \\ a\\b/
#+end_src

#+name: t:0005-styled-strings-escape-marker
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0005-styled-strings-escape-marker"
  $ nilOrgDoc
    [ CParagraph
        $ Prose
          <<Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-escape-marker>>
    ]
    []
#+end_src

#+name: Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-escape-marker
#+begin_src haskell
[ ptext "foo and/or 1/2 bar \\ a\\b" [Italic]
]
#+end_src

** Parsing

Parsing styled strings is somewhat tricky, because of two main problems:

  1) the style markers use the same character for both activation and
     deactivation, and

  2) the styles can be nested or interleaved.

You can have, for example, lines that are /italicized like this but also have
*bold text inside* of it/.

We approach these problems with a simple idea: use a *bit mask*
([[newtype:StyleMask]]) to keep track of markers. Every time we see a starting
marker, we turn on the bit in the mask to activate that style (if it's off), or
turn it off (if it's on), by calling [[f:styleMaskFlip]].

If the input is such that the markers are not balanced, such that by the time we
finish parsing a paragraph (or end of a heading) and the mask still has active
styles in it, we throw an error with [[f:failIfLeftoverActiveStyles]]. That is, we
expect style starting and ending markers to always be balanced by the end of
every paragraph or heading. This deviates from vanilla Org, which does not have
this restriction.

#+name: f:failIfLeftoverActiveStyles
#+begin_src haskell
failIfLeftoverActiveStyles :: OrgParser ()
failIfLeftoverActiveStyles = do
  pState <- get
  let (StyleMask mask) = pState.styleMask
  when (mask /= 0) $ do
    fail
      $ "leftover active styles: "
      <> (show $ maskToStyles $ StyleMask mask)
#+end_src

We don't really care which styles are turned on or off at which point (the style
markers can be used at the beginning, middle, or end of a word --- where a word
is any sequence of non-whitespace characters). The only thing we check is that
by the end of the cell (heading or paragraph), all styles must be turned off.

#+name: ti:0005-styled-strings-messy-boundaries
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0005-styled-strings-messy-boundaries
#+begin_src org
,* *foo *bar baz a*b*c (*ok*) **yes
#+end_src

#+name: t:0005-styled-strings-messy-boundaries
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWithState
  "0005-styled-strings-messy-boundaries"
  ( nilOrgDoc
      [ CHeading
          Heading
            { level = 1,
              section = [1],
              body =
                Prose
                  <<Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-messy-boundaries>>,
              meta = fromList []
            }
      ]
      []
  )
  defaultOrgParserState
    { headingOidStack = [Nothing],
      headingLevel = 1,
      headingSectionNumber = [1]
    }
#+end_src

#+name: Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-messy-boundaries
#+begin_src haskell
[ ptext "foo " [Bold],
  ptext "bar baz a" [],
  ptext "b" [Bold],
  ptext "c (" [],
  ptext "ok" [Bold],
  ptext ") yes" []
]
#+end_src

In [[ti:0005-styled-strings-leftover-active-styles]] we keep parsing to the end of
the heading, expecting to see the ending style marker for bold text, but never
see it. So the failure from [[f:failIfLeftoverActiveStyles]] gets triggered.

#+name: ti:0005-styled-strings-leftover-active-styles
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0005-styled-strings-leftover-active-styles
#+begin_src org
,* foo *bar baz
#+end_src

#+name: t:0005-styled-strings-leftover-active-styles
#+begin_src haskell
shouldFailWithMessage
  "0005-styled-strings-leftover-active-styles"
  "leftover active styles: [Bold]"
  15
#+end_src

Note that as soon as we see a style marker we stylize the text at hand; the
~StyleMask~ is "strict" in this sense. This way, we can start parsing stylized
text without needing to see a corresponding ending marker. This imperative style
is easy to think about.

The algorithm for parsing [[data:StyledText]] requires parsing style markers (and
only style markers), or parsing other text that will be affected by style
markers. Let's first look at the simpler case, that of parsing style markers.

#+name: f:styleMarkerParser
#+begin_src haskell
styleMarkerParser :: OrgParser StyledText
styleMarkerParser = do
  pState <- get

  let mask = pState.styleMask
  c <- MP.satisfy $ isStyleMarker mask
  styleMask <- toStyleMask c

  let newMask = styleMaskFlip mask styleMask -- (ref:call-styleMaskFlip)
  put pState {styleMask = newMask}

  pure
    StyledText
      { body = "",
        style = newMask
      }
#+end_src

All we do is parse a single style marker character, and flip the bit for it as
either on or off, with [[f:styleMaskFlip]] [[(call-styleMaskFlip)]]. Ideally we wouldn't
even bother returning anything at the end of [[f:styleMarkerParser]] (because we've
updated the ~pState~ and that's the only essential thing we must do). But because
of the way it is used in [[f:proseParser]], we have to return a ~StyledText~. We
return an empty ~body~ field though, so that we can filter it out with the
~nonEmpty~ helper in [[f:proseParser]].

We cannot parse multiple style markers in one go, because [[f:isStyleMarker]]
changes behavior depending on the current active style mask. If we are in a
verbatim or code style, we ignore all other style markers except for those that
will end the current (verbatim or code) style. Otherwise, we won't be able to
write stuff like ~*~ because we would think that the asterisk in there is starting
a bold style (which it is not because it is surrounded by ~\~~ to mark code).

#+name: f:isStyleMarker
#+begin_src haskell
isStyleMarker :: StyleMask -> Char -> Bool
isStyleMarker styleMask c
  | isVerbatim styleMask = c == '='
  | isCode styleMask = c == '~'
  | otherwise = member c styleMarkers
#+end_src

Parsing actual styled text requires reading the state of the ~StyleMask~, and then
reading some text. This is done in [[f:styledTextParser]]. It delegates essentially
all of the work to [[f:textParser]].

#+name: f:styledTextParser
#+begin_src haskell
styledTextParser :: OrgParser StyledText
styledTextParser = do
  pState <- get

  let mask = pState.styleMask
  text <- textParser mask

  pure
    StyledText
      { body = text,
        style = mask
      }
#+end_src

The [[f:textParser]] is careful not to step on other parsers' toes, because in a
sense it is the most "greedy" parser, as it can parse many different types of
input.

#+name: f:textParser
#+begin_src haskell
textParser :: StyleMask -> OrgParser Text
textParser styleMask
  | isVerbatim styleMask || isCode styleMask = do
      t <- verbatimCodeParser styleMask
      pure $ T.map newlineToSpace t -- (ref:call-newlineToSpace)
  | otherwise = do
      o <- MP.getOffset
      word <-
        MP.choice
          [ MP.try $ slashSeparatedWords,
            MP.label "normal characters" normalChars,
            escapedSymbolParser styleMask -- (ref:call-escapedSymbolParser)
          ]
      pState <- get
      let haveNotSeenGlossaryTermDelimiterYet =
            pState.insideParagraph && pState.glossaryTermOffset == 0
          wordIsGlossaryTermDelimiter = word == "::" && isEmptyMask styleMask
      when
        ( haveNotSeenGlossaryTermDelimiterYet
            && wordIsGlossaryTermDelimiter
        )
        $ do
          modify (\s -> s {glossaryTermOffset = o}) -- (ref:glossary-term-delimiter-detection)
      pure word
  where
    newlineToSpace c = if c == '\n' then ' ' else c
    normalChars :: OrgParser Text
    normalChars = do
      cs <- MP.some $ normalChar
      pure $ T.pack cs
    normalChar :: OrgParser Char
    normalChar = do
      pState <- get
      let toAvoid =
            [ (pState.insideCitation, string ";"),
              (pState.insideCitePrefix, citeIdParser),
              ( pState.insideCitation
                  && (pState.bracketBalance == 0),
                string "]"
              ),
              (pState.insideLinkDisplayName, string "]]"),
              ( not pState.insideCitation
                  && not pState.insideCitePrefix
                  && not pState.insideLinkDisplayName,
                string "[["
              )
            ]
      forM_
        toAvoid
        $ \(condition, p) -> when condition $ MP.notFollowedBy p
      c <- MP.satisfy isNormalChar
      let bracketBalanceDiff
            | c == '[' = 1
            | c == ']' = -1
            | otherwise = 0
      modify (\s -> s {bracketBalance = s.bracketBalance + bracketBalanceDiff})
      pure c
    isNormalChar c =
      notMember c styleMarkers
        && (not $ isSpace c)
        && (not $ c == '\\')
#+end_src

For the verbatim and code styles, we replace newlines with spaces with calls to
~newlineToSpace~ [[(call-newlineToSpace)]], because in practice newlines can get
inserted only because we're wrapping the source text (in Org mode) at 80
columns. If the user wants to put in a piece of multiline text with specific
newlines, they should just use a source code block instead of an inline verbatim
or code style.

The verbatim and code styles rely on [[f:verbatimCodeParser]] to parse text inside
those styles. We support escaping the closing style marker with a backslash ~\\~,
because this is the only way to allow using the styles with the closing markers.
That is, if we just did a simple

#+begin_src haskell
MP.takeWhile1P (Just "code") (\c -> c /= '~')
#+end_src

then there would be no way to write something like

#+begin_src txt
~~~
#+end_src

to have ~\~~ (tilde) inside a code style. You'd have to write

#+begin_src txt
=~=
#+end_src

to enclose the tilde inside a verbatim style. However, because of
[[(verbatimCodeParser-escapedSymbol)]], we can write

#+begin_src txt
~\~~
#+end_src

to stylize a single tilde with the code style. The disadvantage is that we have
to always escape a backslash ~\\~ if we want just a single backslash without doing
any escaping.

#+name: f:verbatimCodeParser
#+begin_src haskell
verbatimCodeParser :: StyleMask -> OrgParser Text
verbatimCodeParser styleMask = do
  parts <-
    MP.some
      $ MP.choice
        [ escapedSymbol, -- (ref:verbatimCodeParser-escapedSymbol)
          MP.takeWhile1P Nothing isNotClosingOrEscape
        ]
  pure $ T.concat parts
  where
    escapedSymbol :: OrgParser Text
    escapedSymbol = do
      _ <- char '\\'
      c <- MP.satisfy (\c -> not $ isSpace c)
      if
        | isVerbatim styleMask && c `elem` ['=', '\\'] -> pure $ T.singleton c
        | isCode styleMask && c `elem` ['~', '\\'] -> pure $ T.singleton c
        | otherwise -> pure $ "\\" <> T.singleton c
    isNotClosingOrEscape c
      | isVerbatim styleMask = c /= '=' && c /= '\\'
      | otherwise = c /= '~' && c /= '\\'
#+end_src

Back to [[f:textParser]]. We take special care to escape style markers with
[[f:escapedSymbolParser]] [[(call-escapedSymbolParser)]].

#+name: f:escapedSymbolParser
#+begin_src haskell
escapedSymbolParser :: StyleMask -> OrgParser Text
escapedSymbolParser styleMask = do
  _ <- char '\\'
  s <-
    MP.choice
      [ do
          c <- MP.satisfy (\c -> isStyleMarker styleMask c || c == '\\')
          pure $ T.singleton c,
        string "n",
        string "vert{}"
      ]
  pure $ case s of
    "vert{}" -> "|"
    "n" -> "\n"
    _ -> s
#+end_src

[[f:escapedSymbolParser]] also lets users insert a newline character with ~\n~. This
way, you can write multiline text inside a table, even if the table is not
taking up the full width of the HTML page (at which point the browser will
insert soft newlines on its own).

The word ~\vert{}~ can be used to escape a pipe character inside [[data:Table][table]] cells,
because although our Megaparsec-powered parser is powerful enough to handle
escaping pipe characters with ~\|~ (for consistency with ~\/~ to escape the italic
style marker), Orgmode is unable to deal with this in Emacs (so pressing ~TAB~
will mess up the alignment of table cells). For this reason, we use ~\vert{}~
instead. Of course, outside of tables the pipe character can be written raw like
this "|" without issue.

There is one exception to the "style markers must always be balanced" rule, and
it's what we call "slash-separated words". These are cases where we see one or
more slash characters inside an alphanumeric word, such as and/or, or a plain
(non-math) fraction like 1/2, or even something like 1/2/3. In these cases, we
don't want the slashes to be treated as italic style markers.

#+name: f:slashSeparatedWords
#+begin_src haskell
slashSeparatedWords :: OrgParser Text
slashSeparatedWords = do
  let wordParser :: OrgParser Text
      wordParser = do
        _ <- cannotStartWithClosingPunc
        MP.takeWhile1P (Just "slash-separated words") isWordChar
  firstWord <- wordParser
  remainingWords <-
    MP.some $ do
      _ <- MP.notFollowedBy $ MP.choice [string "/ ", string "/\n"]
      _ <- char '/'
      word <- wordParser
      pure $ "/" <> word
  when (lastWordIsMeaningless $ reverse remainingWords)
    $ fail
      """
      last word is not meaningful; unlikely to be
      intended as a slash-separated-word
      """
  let allWords = firstWord : remainingWords
  when (hasLinkDelimiter allWords)
    $ fail
      """
      has link delimiter \"[[\"; unlikely to be
      intended as a slash-separated-word
      """
  pure $ T.concat allWords
  where
    cannotStartWithClosingPunc :: OrgParser ()
    cannotStartWithClosingPunc =
      MP.notFollowedBy
        $ MP.choice
          [ string "]",
            string "}",
            string ")",
            string "]]"
          ]
    isWordChar c = not (isSpace c) && c `notElem` (keys styleMarkers <> "\\")
    lastWordIsMeaningless :: [Text] -> Bool
    lastWordIsMeaningless = \case
      [] -> True
      (lastWord : [])
        | lastWord `elem` ["/.", "/,", "/:"] -> True
        | otherwise -> False
      _ -> False
    hasLinkDelimiter :: [Text] -> Bool
    hasLinkDelimiter = or . map ("[[" `T.isInfixOf`)
#+end_src

The rules inside [[f:slashSeparatedWords]] are a "best effort" attempt, not
something that is always correct. For the cases where we need more flexibility,
you can use escaped slashes like in
[[ti:0005-styled-strings-escape-marker]].

See [[ti:0005-styled-strings-slash-separated-words]] for some real-world examples.

** =StyleMask= details
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       87cd7d62-1f5c-4442-87ff-e1f2a68a8e56
:END:

The previous section explained how we parse styled strings without getting into
the implementation behind the [[newtype:StyleMask]]. We now get into these
implementation details.

The ~StyleMask~ is an 8-bit word, which can hold boolean values for the 6
different ~Style~ data constructors, e.g. [[data:Style][Bold, Italic, etc]]. It's a
space-efficient type to replace an equivalent map or list of ~[Style]~ values. We
use a ~newtype~ around a ~Word8~ (an 8-bit word) so as to keep it distinct from
~Word8~ itself.

#+name: newtype:StyleMask
#+begin_src haskell
type StyleMask :: Type
newtype StyleMask = StyleMask Word8
  deriving stock (Eq, Generic, Ord)
  deriving anyclass (FromJSON, ToJSON)
#+end_src

We have a custom ~Show~ instance because the default ~Show~ would just treat the
~Word8~ as a number. Instead we want to show what the binary bits (turned on
inside the ~Word8~) would represent in terms of actual styles. We have to import
~Prelude~ because of the way ~Relude~ redefines ~show~ (and we need to use the ~show~
that ~Prelude~ uses; see [[https://github.com/snoyberg/basic-prelude/issues/67#issuecomment-221628073][this related discussion for the Basic Prelude library, a
Relude alternative, for reference]]).

#+name: instance:Show Stylemask
#+begin_src haskell
instance Show StyleMask where
  show (StyleMask a) = "styleMaskFrom " <> x
    where
      x =
        Prelude.show
          . catMaybes
          . map
            ( \(i, b) ->
                if b
                  then (Just $ (toEnum i :: Style))
                  else Nothing
            )
          . zip [0 ..]
          $ toListLE a
#+end_src

The shown text string is prefixed with [[f:styleMaskFrom]] because that function is
used as a constructor function to create a ~StyleMask~ easily from scratch. This
follows the tradition used in ~Data.Map~ where they also use a constructor
function (~fromList~) when ~show~-ing a ~Map~ data structure.

#+name: f:styleMaskFrom
#+begin_src haskell
styleMaskFrom :: [Style] -> StyleMask
styleMaskFrom ss = StyleMask $ foldl' f (0 :: Word8) ss
  where
    f :: Word8 -> Style -> Word8
    f acc s = setBit acc (fromEnum s)
#+end_src

Similarly, we sometimes want to go the other direction --- that is, deconstruct
a ~StyleMask~ into ~[Style]~. So we have [[f:maskToStyles]].

#+name: f:maskToStyles
#+begin_src haskell
maskToStyles :: StyleMask -> [Style]
maskToStyles (StyleMask mask) = foldl' f [] $ zip [0 ..] $ toListLE mask
  where
    f :: [Style] -> (Int, Bool) -> [Style]
    f acc (idx, b) =
      if b
        then (toEnum idx) : acc
        else acc
#+end_src

We have a helper function [[f:toStyleMask]] to convert ~Char~ (a style marker
character) into a ~StyleMask~. This is used to [[f:styleMarkerParser][construct a style mask from a
style marker]] in the input Org doc. The ~toStyleMask~ function runs inside a
~MonadFail~ monad, because the ~Char~ type could hold a value that is not a
valid style marker. The use of ~MonadFail~ is seamless inside ~ParsecT~ (the
monad we use inside ~OrgParser~), because ~ParsecT~ has a ~MonadFail~ instance.

#+name: f:toStyleMask
#+begin_src haskell
toStyleMask :: (MonadFail m) => Char -> m StyleMask
toStyleMask cs = pure =<< styleToStyleMask =<< charToStyle cs

charToStyle :: (MonadFail m) => Char -> m Style
charToStyle c = case lookup c styleMarkers of
  Just style -> pure style
  Nothing -> fail $ "programmer error: unrecognized style marker " <> (show c)

styleToStyleMask :: (MonadFail m) => Style -> m StyleMask
styleToStyleMask s = pure $ StyleMask (setBit 0 $ fromEnum s)
#+end_src

[[f:styleMaskFlip]] is a helper to perform a binary XOR operation on two ~StyleMask~
objects, so that callers don't have to perform the deconstruction and
reconstruction of the underlying ~Word8~ primitive themselves. This operation is
used to either turn on bits, or turn off bits if they're already on.

#+name: f:styleMaskFlip
#+begin_src haskell
styleMaskFlip :: StyleMask -> StyleMask -> StyleMask
styleMaskFlip (StyleMask a) (StyleMask b) = StyleMask $ a .^. b
#+end_src

We also have some helpers to check whether particular styles are active.

#+name: f:isVerbatim
#+begin_src haskell
isVerbatim :: StyleMask -> Bool
isVerbatim (StyleMask a) = testBit a (fromEnum Verbatim)
#+end_src

#+name: f:isCode
