#+begin_src haskell
isCode :: StyleMask -> Bool
isCode (StyleMask a) = testBit a (fromEnum Code)
#+end_src

[[f:isEmptyMask]] checks whether any styles are active.

#+name: f:isEmptyMask
#+begin_src haskell
isEmptyMask :: StyleMask -> Bool
isEmptyMask (StyleMask a) = a == 0
#+end_src

We stated earlier around [[t:0005-styled-strings-leftover-active-styles]] that we
don't have to wait to see the ending style marker to start parsing styled text.
While this is true, the downside is that by the time we construct a ~StyledText~,
we have no knowledge of future ~StyledText~ objects that have not yet been parsed.
This can lead to a proliferation of ~StyledText~ objects that all share the same
style, but are still parsed into separate ~PToken~ objects. That is, instead of
just one ~PToken~ with a single ~StyledText~ object with a sizable ~body~ field, we
could end up with ~[PToken]~ where each of the elements has a small ~body~, but with
the same overall style.

Obviously, having a list of ~PToken~ objects when we can just have a single one is
problematic, if not wasteful. We want to join up sequences of styled text that
share the same style, and make them use a single ~StyledText~ object. For this
purpose, there is [[f:compressProse]].

#+name: f:compressProse
#+begin_src haskell
compressProse :: Prose -> Prose
compressProse prose =
  Prose
    . reverse
    . dropUnnecessary -- (ref:dropUnnecessary)
    $ foldl' f [] prose.unProse
  where
    f :: [PToken] -> PToken -> [PToken]
    f [] token = token : []
    f
      ((t1@(PTokenStyledText (StyledText body1 style1))) : rest)
      t2@(PTokenStyledText (StyledText body2 style2))
        | style1 == style2 =
            (PTokenStyledText $ StyledText (T.concat [body1, body2]) style1)
              : rest
        | otherwise = t2 : t1 : rest
    f acc token = token : acc
    dropUnnecessary [] = []
    dropUnnecessary (((PTokenStyledText (StyledText " " _))) : rest) = rest
    dropUnnecessary (((PTokenStyledText (StyledText "" _))) : rest) = rest
    dropUnnecessary tokens = tokens
#+end_src

This function works by looking at two consecutive ~PTokenStyledText~ elements at a
time (one that's already in the accumulator (~acc~) from the previous iteration
and one from the current ~token~ at hand), joining them if they share the same
style.

[[f:compressProse]] also eliminates trailing whitespace, with ~dropUnnecessary~
[[(dropUnnecessary)]]. Trailing whitespace can occur if the last word of a paragraph
at the end of the file ends with an active ~StyleMask~ followed by a newline
(trailing whitespace between paragraphs can't occur because the ~lexeme~ in
[[f:cellParser]] will skip past them). For example, if the input text looks like

#+begin_src org
_foo
bar_
#+end_src

then the last underscore can be followed by a newline (assuming the editor used
to edit this file is UNIX-friendly and appends a trailing newline), in which
case that newline would get parsed by the ~spaceConsumer~ in [[f:paragraphParser]] as
its own separate ~StyledText~ object as a single space character. We want to
eliminate these objects because they don't add any useful information for Lilac
itself, and so we use ~dropUnnecessary~ above to filter them out.

~dropUnnecessary~ also removes tokens with an empty string as the ~StyledText~
object. This can occur when we parse trailing newlines at the end of the line
even without style markers, from [[f:whitespaceCompressor]].

Here we test how we minimize the number of ~PToken~ objects; although the
individual letters are spaced out with multiple spaces, we only use two ~PToken~
objects (one for the plain text and another for the bold text) and each of these
objects have just single spaces between the letters.

#+name: ti:0005-styled-strings-compression
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0005-styled-strings-compression
#+begin_src org
a b  c   d    e      *f g  h   i    j*
#+end_src

#+name: t:0005-styled-strings-compression
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0005-styled-strings-compression"
  $ nilOrgDoc
    [ CParagraph
        $ Prose
          <<Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-compression>>
    ]
    []
#+end_src

#+name: Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-compression
#+begin_src haskell
[ ptext "a b c d e " [],
  ptext "f g h i j" [Bold]
]
#+end_src

[[f:compressStyledText]] is like [[f:compressProse]], but only for [[data:StyledText]]. It's
used in [[id:7874b9c5-ceac-4820-8804-7732e29f5c60::f:cslJsonTextToProse][f:cslJsonTextToProse]] to cut down on the verbosity of ~StyledText~ items
generated by the conversion of ~CslJson~ to ~Prose~.

#+name: f:compressStyledText
#+begin_src haskell
compressStyledText :: [StyledText] -> [StyledText]
compressStyledText = reverse . dropUnnecessary . foldl' f []
  where
    f :: [StyledText] -> StyledText -> [StyledText]
    f [] styledText = styledText : []
    f
      ((t1@(StyledText body1 style1)) : rest)
      t2@(StyledText body2 style2)
        | style1 == style2 =
            (StyledText (T.concat [body1, body2]) style1) : rest
        | otherwise = t2 : t1 : rest
    dropUnnecessary [] = []
    dropUnnecessary ((StyledText " " _) : rest) = rest
    dropUnnecessary ((StyledText "" _) : rest) = rest
    dropUnnecessary styledTexts = styledTexts
#+end_src

** Additional tests

The simple case with no style markers.

#+name: ti:0005-styled-strings-no-style
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0005-styled-strings-no-style
#+begin_src org
foo
#+end_src

#+name: t:0005-styled-strings-no-style
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0005-styled-strings-no-style"
  $ nilOrgDoc
    [ plainParagraph "foo"
    ]
    []
#+end_src

The next case tests a simple case where one word is in bold.

#+name: ti:0005-styled-strings-bold
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0005-styled-strings-bold
#+begin_src org
foo *bar* baz
#+end_src

#+name: t:0005-styled-strings-bold
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0005-styled-strings-bold"
  $ nilOrgDoc
    [ CParagraph
        $ Prose
          [ ptext "foo " [],
            ptext "bar" [Bold],
            ptext " baz" []
          ]
    ]
    []
#+end_src

Below we mix things up a bit more, using more styles.

#+name: ti:0005-styled-strings-complex
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0005-styled-strings-complex
#+begin_src org
,*foo /bar _underlined  bold italic_/ baz* =verbatim  text= ~x  y  z~ *bold*
#+end_src

#+name: t:0005-styled-strings-complex
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0005-styled-strings-complex"
  $ nilOrgDoc
    [ CParagraph
        $ Prose
          <<Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-complex>>
    ]
    []
#+end_src

#+name: Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-complex
#+begin_src haskell
[ ptext "foo " [Bold],
  ptext "bar " [Bold, Italic],
  ptext "underlined bold italic" [Bold, Italic, Underline],
  ptext " baz" [Bold],
  ptext " " [],
  ptext "verbatim  text" [Verbatim],
  ptext " " [],
  ptext "x  y  z" [Code],
  ptext " " [],
  ptext "bold" [Bold]
]
#+end_src

Here's another test, which checks some edge cases when dealing with punctuation.

#+name: ti:0005-styled-strings-complex-punctuation
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0005-styled-strings-complex-punctuation
#+begin_src org
(foo =bar=). What's up? and *that!* /(italic)/. It's #1!
#+end_src

#+name: t:0005-styled-strings-complex-punctuation
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0005-styled-strings-complex-punctuation"
  $ nilOrgDoc
    [ CParagraph
        $ Prose
          <<Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-complex-punctuation>>
    ]
    []
#+end_src

Note how we preserve the information of having no space character between the
=bar= and the closing parenthesis ")".

#+name: Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-complex-punctuation
#+begin_src haskell
[ ptext "(foo " [],
  ptext "bar" [Verbatim],
  ptext "). What's up? and " [],
  ptext "that!" [Bold],
  ptext " " [],
  ptext "(italic)" [Italic],
  ptext ". It's #1!" []
]
#+end_src

Check that we can parse slashes inside words as slashes, not as italic style
markers.

#+name: ti:0005-styled-strings-slash-separated-words
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0005-styled-strings-slash-separated-words
#+begin_src org
/italic fraction 1/2/ foo and/or bar /A+/B-/ +1/+2. key/value
#+end_src

#+name: t:0005-styled-strings-slash-separated-words
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0005-styled-strings-slash-separated-words"
  $ nilOrgDoc
    [ CParagraph
        $ Prose
          <<Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-slash-separated-words>>
    ]
    []
#+end_src

#+name: Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-slash-separated-words
#+begin_src haskell
[ ptext "italic fraction 1/2" [Italic],
  ptext " foo and/or bar " [],
  ptext "A+/B-" [Italic],
  ptext " +1/+2. key/value" []
]
#+end_src

This is another test, involving the ~Verbatim~ style. It checks the case where we
have a style marker inside a word (not at the usual beginning or end of a word).

#+name: ti:0005-styled-strings-verbatim
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0005-styled-strings-verbatim
#+begin_src org
(=import ./.=) hello
#+end_src

#+name: t:0005-styled-strings-verbatim
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0005-styled-strings-verbatim"
  $ nilOrgDoc
    [ CParagraph
        $ Prose
          <<Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-verbatim>>
    ]
    []
#+end_src

#+name: Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-verbatim
#+begin_src haskell
[ ptext "(" [],
  ptext "import ./." [Verbatim],
  ptext ") hello" []
]
#+end_src

This is another test to check that the bold style marker (asterisk) is ignored
if inside a verbatim or code marker.

#+name: ti:0005-styled-strings-verbatim-code-ignore-immediate-bold
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0005-styled-strings-verbatim-code-ignore-immediate-bold
#+begin_src org
=*a= =b*= ~*c~ ~d*~
#+end_src

#+name: t:0005-styled-strings-verbatim-code-ignore-immediate-bold
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0005-styled-strings-verbatim-code-ignore-immediate-bold"
  $ nilOrgDoc
    [ CParagraph
        $ Prose
          <<Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-verbatim-code-ignore-immediate-bold>>
    ]
    []
#+end_src

#+name: Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-verbatim-code-ignore-immediate-bold
#+begin_src haskell
[ ptext "*a" [Verbatim],
  ptext " " [],
  ptext "b*" [Verbatim],
  ptext " " [],
  ptext "*c" [Code],
  ptext " " [],
  ptext "d*" [Code]
]
#+end_src

Verbatim and code styles take precedence over [[id:f8254d58-7a3d-4595-a265-e0d6255e6ab5][links]]. So even if you have text
that looks like a link, they will not be parsed as such if surrounded by
verbatim or code styles. The same applies for [[id:216e94b7-e037-4f3e-856a-2040d0d0a75a][inline math]], whose parser should
not apply if we're inside a verbatim (or code) region.

#+name: ti:0005-styled-strings-verbatim-precedence
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0005-styled-strings-verbatim-precedence
#+begin_src org
=[[foo=

[[hello]] there

=\(1 + 1 = ~\(2 * 2\)~

~[[bar]]~
#+end_src

#+name: t:0005-styled-strings-verbatim-precedence
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0005-styled-strings-verbatim-precedence"
  $ nilOrgDoc
    <<Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-verbatim-precedence>>
    [PseudoEdge (LCI 1, LinkOid {oid = fromWords 0 0, name = "hello"})]
#+end_src

#+name: Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-verbatim-precedence
#+begin_src haskell
[ CParagraph
    $ Prose
      [ ptext "[[foo" [Verbatim]
      ],
  CParagraph
    $ Prose
      [ PTokenLink
          Link
            { target =
                LinkTargetOid
                  LinkOid
                    { oid = nilOid,
                      name = "hello"
                    },
              name =
                [ stext "hello" []
                ]
            },
        ptext " there" []
      ],
  CParagraph
    $ Prose
      [ ptext "\\(1 + 1 " [Verbatim],
        ptext " " [],
        ptext "\\(2 * 2\\)" [Code]
      ],
  CParagraph
    $ Prose
      [ ptext "[[bar]]" [Code]
      ]
]
#+end_src

[[ti:0005-styled-strings-escaping]] checks how we handle symbol escaping inside
verbatim and code styles (compare against [[f:verbatimCodeParser]]).

#+name: ti:0005-styled-strings-escaping
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0005-styled-strings-escaping
#+begin_src org
=\\= =\== =\(= ~\\~ ~\~~ ~\(~
#+end_src

#+name: t:0005-styled-strings-escaping
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0005-styled-strings-escaping"
  $ nilOrgDoc
    <<Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-escaping>>
    []
#+end_src

#+name: Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-escaping
#+begin_src haskell
[ CParagraph
    $ Prose
      [ ptext "\\" [Verbatim],
        ptext " " [],
        ptext "=" [Verbatim],
        ptext " " [],
        ptext "\\(" [Verbatim],
        ptext " " [],
        ptext "\\" [Code],
        ptext " " [],
        ptext "~" [Code],
        ptext " " [],
        ptext "\\(" [Code]
      ]
]
#+end_src

* Links
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       f8254d58-7a3d-4595-a265-e0d6255e6ab5
:END:

A link has

  - a name :: what's displayed to the reader, and

  - a target :: where we want to take the reader when they click on the link.

#+name: data:Link
#+begin_src haskell
type Link :: Type
data Link = Link
  { name :: [StyledText],
    target :: LinkTarget
  }
  deriving stock (Eq, Generic, Ord, Show)
  deriving anyclass (FromJSON, ToJSON)
#+end_src

The [[data:LinkTarget]] is a rich definition of the kind of target we are linking
to. For now, this data structure only supports the following cases:

  1) [[data:LinkOid]], which links to either another part of the current Org
     document or another (external) Org document. "External" doesn't mean any
     Org document; it must be compiled inside the same [[id:25827c17-2015-434b-b686-db3d72b2ee4d::data:Archive][data:Archive]].

     These are also used for Noweb reference inside another code block (because
     code blocks are just cells). They can be used to also link to figures,
     which are also cells. [[fig:PNG image][Example]]

     When inside code blocks, these use angle brackets (e.g., ~<<foo>>~). This can
     reference code blocks in other files by the [[id:96f8cbda-abca-48bd-bb89-512aaac61ca8][Org ID (OID)]], where the syntax
     is ~<<id:...::foo>>~ where ~...~ is the OID and ~foo~ is the name of the code
     block at that OID (either using the top-level OID of the document or the
     OID of the closest enclosing heading). We have to use an OID-to-file map
     data structure (which is parsed [[id:96f8cbda-abca-48bd-bb89-512aaac61ca8][here]]).

     When used outside of code blocks, square brackets are used (e.g.,
     ~[[target]]~ or ~[[target][name]]~) where ~target~ is just a plain text string
     (such as a URL or some other entity in the document), and ~name~ is what is
     displayed to the user when the link is rendered.

  2) A [[id:da4aa2a7-3896-4969-aa5e-5e3d842c35db][line label]], which is a link that points to a reference /inside/ a code
     block. This is useful for referring to different areas inside a code block,
     without having to break up the code block into multiple separate parts.

  3) A [[id:4127feee-755d-430e-8fef-192e95143790][footnote definition]]. These also use [[data:LinkOid]] internally,
     and always refer to footnote definitions within the same Org document.

  4) URI (a typical web link, most common example being ~https://...~).

  5) File path. These links are only useful because they let you go open that
     file on disk locally in Emacs. On weaving these links are checked if they
     refer to a path inside the current Git repo. If they are inside, then we
     calculate what the link /should/ target (based on the weaving configuration),
     assuming that the entire Git repo is also being served somewhere on the
     internet.

     If the file path is pointing to a place outside the Git repo, then upon
     weaving we display it as a path description (and not as a link), because
     the link will be broken anyway (again, assuming that only the content
     inside the Git repo is accessible by a link).

There are also [[id:8fe81206-f50d-4200-a95e-99278e0ad410][Raw links]] which is prose text that looks like a URL. For example,
https://www.haskell.org is an example of a raw link. This is a convenience
facility because ~[[https://www.haskell.org]]~ behaves the same way as
~https://www.haskell.org~.

#+name: data:LinkTarget
#+begin_src haskell
type LinkTarget :: Type
data LinkTarget
  = LinkTargetOid LinkOid
  | LinkTargetLineLabel LinkOid
  | LinkTargetFootnote Text
  | LinkTargetURI N.URI
  | LinkTargetFilePath FilePath -- (ref:data:LinkTarget-LinkTargetFilePath)
  deriving stock (Eq, Generic, Ord, Show)
  deriving anyclass (FromJSON, ToJSON)
#+end_src

The [[data:LinkOid]] is made up of the Org ID and the name. This is used pervasively
as it is the only way to refer to headings (within the same doc or in an
external Org doc). It is also required for constructing Noweb links that refer
to code blocks from external documents. See [[f:oidReferenceParser]] to see the
expected format of these links.

#+name: data:LinkOid
#+begin_src haskell
type LinkOid :: Type
data LinkOid = LinkOid
  { oid :: OID,
    name :: Text -- (ref:data:LinkOid-name)
  }
  deriving stock (Eq, Generic, Ord)
#+end_src

We have a custom ~Show~ instance for [[data:LinkOid]] so that it's much more concise
(especially when we're marshaling and unmarshaling from JSON).

#+name: instance:Show LinkOid
#+begin_src haskell
instance Show LinkOid where
  show (LinkOid (OID uuid) name)
    | T.null name = "id:" <> show uuid
    | otherwise = "id:" <> show uuid <> "::" <> T.unpack name
#+end_src

** OIDs and Emacs
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       96f8cbda-abca-48bd-bb89-512aaac61ca8
:END:

Lilac relies on Org files that are written in Emacs (as it is the best
environment for writing Org files). When the ~org-id-locations-file~ and
~org-id-track-globally~ variables are set, Emacs remembers which files have which
Org IDs (where the "ID" is the OID generated by ~org-id-get-create~ and
~org-id-store-link~). Emacs then saves this information to the value of
~org-id-locations-file~.

Historically Lilac used to parse these files generated by Emacs, but it has
moved on to a compilation pipeline that doesn't need to rely on these files
coming from Emacs. If Lilac wants to bring back tangling and weaving based
purely on Org files without the compilation process, it will be forced to read
these OID location files again. Below is a brief discussion of the format of
these files to serve as a historical reference.

The syntax is of a list of list of strings, in Emacs lisp. The inner lists have
the relative file path of the Org file as the first element, followed by the
OIDs. Each Org ID is just a UUIDv4 string. The OIDs are in reverse order
(probably because Emacs grows the list right-to-left, just like Haskell's ~:~ (aka
"cons") function).

Below is an example, with newlines inserted for better presentation (Emacs does
not use newlines here, and so in the wild everything is in one line):

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(("note/raw/recreation.org" "a1e6d0d5-4540-423b-864e-b96ad06c56eb")
 ("../prog/lilac/index.org" "3c576a02-418b-431b-ab96-e4936e977fb7"
                            "ce9af5ef-6559-4ffb-82c1-a439214eda6d"
                            "8f5752c0-6162-4de6-9ceb-f78ba584722c"
                            "b449a097-d702-4fd6-98de-cb81f7e49177"))
#+end_src

The information stored in this file is global, because Emacs will update it
whenever it encounters Org files.

** Standalone link parser

The standalone link parser [[f:linkParser]] can parse links that can be embedded
inside prose text. In practice this is probably the most important link parser
because it knows how to parse links found in prose text (outside of code
blocks).

#+name: f:linkParser
#+begin_src haskell
linkParser :: OrgParser Link
linkParser = do
  failIfVerbatimOrCode

  MP.choice
    [ MP.try oidLinkParser,
      MP.try protocolWebLinkParser,
      MP.try fileLinkParser,
      MP.try footnoteLinkParser,
      MP.try rawLinkParser
    ]
#+end_src

The call to [[f:failIfVerbatimOrCode]] fails the parser if either the [[f:isVerbatim][verbatim or
code style is active]]. This is because the verbatim and code styles take
precedence over parsing links. See, for example
[[t:0005-styled-strings-verbatim-precedence]] of the sort of behavior we expect.

#+name: f:failIfVerbatimOrCode
#+begin_src haskell
failIfVerbatimOrCode :: OrgParser ()
failIfVerbatimOrCode = do
  pState <- get
  let mask = pState.styleMask
  when (isVerbatim mask || isCode mask)
    $ fail "aborting because verbatim or code style is active"
#+end_src

By conditionally failing [[f:linkParser]] with [[f:failIfVerbatimOrCode]], we also allow
other parsers (such as [[f:styledTextParser]]) to succeed (see [[f:proseParser]] for the
interaction of this parser with these other parsers).

Note that in [[f:linkParser]] we don't parse [[id:81e1c841-5de8-4111-9b18-f33bea904122][Noweb links]] here because they can only
exist inside [[id:cae993e5-02bd-4f89-a6d3-20b41adaff8f][code blocks]].

** OID links
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       dabccd5e-930e-4571-899f-1bfbdeab3d5e
:END:

The [[f:oidLinkParser]] closely resembles the [[f:nowebLinkTargetParser]], because they
both encode a [[data:LinkOid]], the only difference being where they are used (Noweb
links are used exclusively inside code blocks to refer to other code blocks).

#+name: f:oidLinkParser
#+begin_src haskell
oidLinkParser :: OrgParser Link
oidLinkParser = do
  _ <- string "[["
  avoidParsingKnownProtocols
  (oid, name) <- oidReferenceParser "[]" "]]" -- (ref:f:oidLinkParser-oidReferenceParser)
  pState <- get
  let mask = pState.styleMask
      implicitDisplayName
        | T.null name = "id:" <> T.show oid
        | otherwise = name
      implicitDisplayNameStyledText
        | isLineLabel name = []
        | otherwise =
            [ StyledText
                { body = implicitDisplayName,
                  style = mask
                }
            ]

  _ <- string "]"
  displayName <-
    MP.choice
      [ MP.try $ string "]" >> pure implicitDisplayNameStyledText,
        MP.try linkDisplayNameParser -- (ref:f:oidLinkParser-linkDisplayNameParser)
      ]
  let linkOid =
        LinkOid
          { oid = oid,
            name = name
          }
      linkTargetConstructor =
        if isLineLabel name
          then LinkTargetLineLabel -- (ref:f:oidLinkParser-line-label-constructor)
          else LinkTargetOid
      edge = PseudoEdge (pState.cellNumber, linkOid) -- (ref:f:oidLinkParser-PseudoEdge)
  modify (\s -> s {OrgParserState.pseudoEdges = edge : s.pseudoEdges})
  pure
    $ Link
      { target = linkTargetConstructor linkOid,
        name = displayName
      }
  where
    avoidParsingKnownProtocols :: OrgParser ()
    avoidParsingKnownProtocols =
      MP.notFollowedBy
        . MP.choice
        . map (MP.try . string)
        $ protocolsWeb
        <> protocolsEmacs
#+end_src

There are three main pieces involved here:

  1) [[f:oidReferenceParser]] [[(f:oidLinkParser-oidReferenceParser)]] for parsing the
     location of the linked item (anything that can be named, such as a code
     block, line label, figure, or table);

  2) [[f:linkDisplayNameParser]] [[(f:oidLinkParser-linkDisplayNameParser)]] for parsing
     the display name of the link (if any); and

  3) so-called "pseudo" edges [[(f:oidLinkParser-PseudoEdge)]].

We keep the opening and closing parentheses from the name of a line label
because even though the ~LinkTargetLineLabel~ value constructor at
[[(f:oidLinkParser-line-label-constructor)]] captures whether the outer ~LinkOid~
belongs to a line label, it's still useful to differentiate it against another
~LinkOid~ with an identical name. For example, if a document has a cell named ~foo~
and a line label like ~(ref:foo)~, we would like to store these as ~LinkOid~ names
~foo~ and ~(foo)~ respectively. See [[id:72f268f8-5703-4f3f-b821-62713149da64::ti:0401-broken-links-line-label][ti:0401-broken-links-line-label]] for a case where
we have a line label link, but no corresponding line label reference in a code
block.

So in practice although we can just pattern match on this constructor to know
whether the [[data:LinkOid]] contained within it is a line label or not, we still
have to inspect the name (with [[f:isLineLabel]]) if we're only dealing with the
inner ~LinkOid~ type.

Pseudo-edges are needed for [[id:b1057136-48d5-402e-8e55-5a7e6a08efe4][backlink]] generation. Now let's look at the first two
pieces in more detail.

*** OID references
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       80637e01-1065-40b0-932c-8ffa2376c863
:END:

The OID reference uniquely identifies the linked item. Below are some examples:

#+begin_src txt
[[foo]]

[[id:62a7d69a-7aa7-4607-b93e-7ed382a5f256][link display name]]

[[id:62a7d69a-7aa7-4607-b93e-7ed382a5f256::target name][link display name]]
#+end_src

The first one, ~[[foo]]~, is called an /implicit/ link; it links to some other cell
(block, table, figure, etc) within the /current/ document, without (explicitly)
specifying the OID of the current document. All documents must have an OID, and
it is parsed in [[f:docMetaParser]]. Implicit links only specify the target (in this
example, ~foo~.

The second one, ~[[id:62a7d69a-7aa7-4607-b93e-7ed382a5f256][link display name]]~,
refers to an OID explicitly. The OID could be either a document itself (the
[[(data:OrgDoc-oid)]]) or the OID of a heading (the latter is much more common in
practice). Note that these OIDs can be any OID, not just from the current
document. This example also has a ~link display name~ as the text to display to
the user when we weave this to HTML. These link display names are parsed
separately in [[f:linkDisplayNameParser]] ([[id:4ae6e928-44be-47a0-baf4-8b3893a7a8fb][Link display names]]).

The third example has a ~target name~ in it. This name may or may not have
newlines in it.

For all three examples, the part in the first set of brackets (~foo~,
~id:62a7d69a-7aa7-4607-b93e-7ed382a5f256~, and
~id:62a7d69a-7aa7-4607-b93e-7ed382a5f256::name of target item~) are all parsed by
[[f:oidReferenceParser]].

#+name: f:oidReferenceParser
#+begin_src haskell
oidReferenceParser :: [Char] -> [Char] -> OrgParser (OID, Text)
oidReferenceParser disallowedChars ending =
  MP.choice
    [ MP.try explicitOIDandTargetName,
      MP.try explicitOID,
      MP.try implicitOIDandExplicitTargetName
    ]
  where
    implicitOIDandExplicitTargetName :: OrgParser (OID, Text)
    implicitOIDandExplicitTargetName = do
      pState <- get
      name <- targetName
      pure (pState.docOid, name) -- (ref:f:oidReferenceParser-docOid)
    explicitOID = do
      _ <- string "id:" -- (ref:f:oidReferenceParser-explicit-oid)
      oidText <-
        MP.some $ do
          _ <- MP.notFollowedBy . string $ T.pack ending
          MP.noneOf $ ':' : disallowedChars
      oid <- validateOidText $ T.pack oidText
      pure (oid, "")
    explicitOIDandTargetName = do
      (oid, _) <- explicitOID
      _ <- string "::"
      name <- targetName
      pure (oid, name)
    targetName :: OrgParser Text
    targetName = compressedTextParser disallowedChars ending -- (ref:f:oidReferenceParser-target-name)
#+end_src

The implicit OID links get their OID assigned for the current document through
the ~pState.docOid~ [[(f:oidReferenceParser-docOid)]], and use ~targetName~
[[(f:oidReferenceParser-target-name)]] to parse the name of the target. We cannot
use the OID of the closest heading (like ~block.parentOid~ for blocks
[[(f:blockParser-firstParentOid)]]) here, because for an internal Noweb link we
would almost always be referring to /another different/ heading in the doc which
would most likely have a different OID than the ~parentOid~. So the best one to
use is ~docOid~. See [[id:ff5876bf-a144-4a08-bba4-15638e3590d5::f:buildGraph][f:buildGraph]] for how the ~docOid~ (aka document's OID) is used
for generating a graph for cycle detection.

For links with an explicit OID, the OID is prefixed with ~id:~
[[(f:oidReferenceParser-explicit-oid)]].

The parsing of the target name is a little bit tricky, because sometimes the
user may want to break long lines with whitespace. This is why
[[f:compressedTextParser]] is more lenient about how whitespace is interpreted, so
that if the user wants to refer to a target that has spaces in it, they can
write that target over multiple lines. That is, a user may refer to a target
name like ~foo bar baz~ in the following ways:

#+begin_src org
[[foo bar
baz]]

[[foo
bar
baz]]

[[foo
bar baz]]
#+end_src

All of the above links refer to the same ~foo bar baz~ named target in the current
document, even though they are written slightly differently.

#+name: f:compressedTextParser
#+begin_src haskell
compressedTextParser :: [Char] -> [Char] -> OrgParser Text
compressedTextParser disallowedChars ending = do
  pState <- get
  tn <-
    proseParser'
      $ proseSpaceConsumer'
        pState.insideCodeBlock
  pure $ compressWhitespace tn
  where
    compressWhitespace = T.unwords . T.words
    proseSpaceConsumer' :: Bool -> OrgParser Text -- (ref:proseSpaceConsumer')
    proseSpaceConsumer' insideCodeBlock
      | insideCodeBlock = onlySpaces
      | otherwise =
          MP.choice
            [ string "\n",
              onlySpaces
            ]
    proseParser' :: OrgParser Text -> OrgParser Text
    proseParser' spaceConsumer = do
      a <- MP.choice baseParsers
      b <- MP.many $ MP.choice (spaceParser : baseParsers)
      pure . T.concat $ a : b
      where
        spaceParser = whitespaceCompressor spaceConsumer
        baseParsers =
          [ textParser'
          ]
    textParser' :: OrgParser Text
    textParser' = do
      ws <-
        MP.some
          $ MP.choice
            [ MP.label "normal characters" normalChars
            ]
      pure $ T.concat ws
      where
        normalChars :: OrgParser Text
        normalChars = do
          cs <- MP.some $ normalChar
          pure $ T.pack cs
        normalChar :: OrgParser Char
        normalChar = do
          _ <- MP.notFollowedBy . string $ T.pack ending
          MP.satisfy isNormalChar
        isNormalChar c =
          notElem c disallowedChars
            && (not $ isSpace c)
#+end_src

Unlike [[f:proseSpaceConsumer]], the ~proseSpaceConsumer'~ [[(proseSpaceConsumer')]] does
*not* check for the correctness of the indentation following a newline (it does
not take an ~expectedIndentLen~ parameter). This is because we want to be more
generous for the case where OID links are found in prose, because sometimes
indentation can get silently added when using ~fill-paragraph~ in Emacs (if the
OID link target gets unlucky and ends up getting hard wrapped by
~fill-paragraph~). Otherwise, this parser will fail, which would then make the OID
link become wrongly parsed.

Now we could punish the user for having erroneous indentation, but because
~org-toggle-link-display~ can hide this error caused by ~fill-paragraph~ (and
because the link target contents will be hidden), we take the approach of being
a bit lenient here. We still disallow newlines inside the link target inside
code blocks, which is why ~insideCodeBlock~ is still a parameter for
~proseSpaceConsumer'~. See [[ti:0006-links-oids-lenient-whitespace]].

*** Link display names
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       4ae6e928-44be-47a0-baf4-8b3893a7a8fb
:END:

[[f:linkDisplayNameParser]] just gets the name of the text to display. It is reused
in [[f:protocolWebLinkParser]]. It uses the ~proseParser'~ [[(proseParser' helper)]]
helper which is very similar to the [[f:proseParser]], except that instead of
returning [[data:PToken]] values, it returns the finer-grained [[data:StyledText]]. We
want to be careful about how we treat whitespace (especially across indentation
levels), so we reuse the same [[f:proseSpaceConsumer]] that [[f:paragraphParser]] uses.

#+name: f:linkDisplayNameParser
#+begin_src haskell
linkDisplayNameParser :: OrgParser [StyledText]
linkDisplayNameParser = do
  pState <- get
  modify (\s -> s {insideLinkDisplayName = True})
  linkDisplayName <-
    MP.between
      (string "[")
      (string "]]")
      (proseParser' $ proseSpaceConsumer pState.continuationIndent)
  modify (\s -> s {insideLinkDisplayName = False})
  pure linkDisplayName
  where
    proseParser' :: OrgParser Text -> OrgParser [StyledText] -- (ref:proseParser' helper)
    proseParser' spaceConsumer = do
      a <- MP.choice baseParsers
      b <- MP.many $ MP.choice (spaceParser : baseParsers)
      pure . filter nonEmpty $ a : b
      where
        spaceParser = do
          s <- whitespaceCompressor spaceConsumer
          pure StyledText {body = s, style = styleMaskFrom []}
        baseParsers =
          [ styleMarkerParser,
            styledTextParser
          ]
        nonEmpty :: StyledText -> Bool
        nonEmpty styledText = not $ T.null styledText.body
#+end_src

*** Tests

Here are some basic links.

#+name: ti:0006-links
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0006-links
#+begin_src org
foo [[link-target-only]]

,* [[link-target][link-name]]

[[a]] b
#+end_src

#+name: t:0006-links

shouldParseWithState

  ( nilOrgDoc
      [ CParagraph
          $ Prose
            [ ptext "foo " [],

                  { target =

                            name = "link-target-only"

                    name = [stext "link-target-only" []]
                  }

            { level = 1,
              section = [1],
              body =
                Prose
                  [ PTokenLink

                        { target =
                            LinkTargetOid
                              LinkOid
                                { oid = nilOid,
                                  name = "link-target"
                                },
                          name = [stext "link-name" []]

              meta = fromList []

        CParagraph
          $ Prose
            [ PTokenLink

                  { target =
                      LinkTargetOid

                            name = "a"

              ptext " b" []
            ]
      ]
      [ PseudoEdge (LCI 2, LinkOid {oid = fromWords 0x0000000000000000 0x0000000000000000, name = "a"}),
        PseudoEdge (LCI 0, LinkOid {oid = fromWords 0 0, name = "link-target-only"})

    { headingOidStack = [Nothing],
      headingLevel = 1,

Sometimes we have text that look like links initially but turn out to be
non-links.

#+name: ti:0006-links-nonlink
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0006-links-nonlink
#+begin_src org
[hi]
#+end_src

#+name: t:0006-links-nonlink
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0006-links-nonlink"
  $ nilOrgDoc
    [ CParagraph $ Prose [ptext "[hi]" []]
    ]
    []
#+end_src

Bracketed links can be preceded by ~(~ which means [[f:linkParser]] won't be
able to parse it directly; however, in [[f:textParser]] (specifically ~normalChar~) we
take care to ensure to stop consuming input if we see a double left bracket ~[[~.
This way, the input will look like ~[[...~ which will then allow
[[f:linkParser]] to parse it. See [[ti:0006-links-inside-parentheses]] for an example.

[[ti:0006-links-oids]] has some more complex input.

#+name: ti:0006-links-oids
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0006-links-oids
#+begin_src org
[[id:442c807f-c9d9-45a7-88bf-ffffffffffff]]
[[id:442c807f-c9d9-45a7-88bf-ffffffffffff][bar]]
[[id:442c807f-c9d9-45a7-88bf-ffffffffffff][bar =[]=]]
[[id:442c807f-c9d9-45a7-88bf-ffffffffffff][bar
ok]]
[[id:442c807f-c9d9-45a7-88bf-ffffffffffff::foo][abc]]
[[foo][abc]]

  - link name across list indentation [[foo][/italic/
    def ghi]
    !]]

  - link target with spaces [[foo bar
    baz]]

  - oid with spaces [[id:442c807f-c9d9-45a7-88bf-ffffffffffff::a b
    c]]
#+end_src

If we encounter a link in the middle of a paragraph (where the ~OID~ portion is
broken up incorrectly such that there is additional whitespace at the beginning
of a line), we should still be able to parse the ~\n ~ as a single space
character because that's almost always the intent. This situation can happen
when invoking ~fill-paragraph~ in Emacs with ~org-toggle-link-display~.

So in [[ti:0006-links-oids-lenient-whitespace]], even though the string (if we parse
it naively) should look like ~with\n newline~, that ~\n ~ will get compressed so
that we just get ~with newline~ instead.

#+name: ti:0006-links-oids-lenient-whitespace
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0006-links-oids-lenient-whitespace
#+begin_src org
Hello [[id:442c807f-c9d9-45a7-88bf-ffffffffffff::target name with
 newline and space is still treated as space][foo]]
#+end_src

#+name: t:0006-links-oids-lenient-whitespace
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0006-links-oids-lenient-whitespace"
  ( nilOrgDoc
      [ CParagraph
          $ Prose
            [ ptext "Hello " [],
              PTokenLink
                Link
                  { target =
                      LinkTargetOid
                        LinkOid
                          { oid = fromWords 0x442c807fc9d945a7 0x88bfffffffffffff,
                            name = "target name with newline and space is still treated as space"
                          },
                    name = [stext "foo" []]
                  }
            ]
      ]
      [PseudoEdge (LCI 0, LinkOid {oid = fromWords 0x442c807fc9d945a7 0x88bfffffffffffff, name = "target name with newline and space is still treated as space"})]
  )
#+end_src

A link to a line label will only have a link display name if the user defines it
explicitly. That is, ~[[(foo)]]~ won't have a name
[[(t:0006-links-oids-line-labels-implicit-blank)]], but ~[[(foo)][explicit name]]~
will have one [[(t:0006-links-oids-line-labels-explicit)]].

#+name: ti:0006-links-oids-line-labels
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0006-links-oids-line-labels
#+header: :line-label off
#+begin_src org
,#+begin_src txt
(ref:foo)
,#+end_src

[[(foo)]] [[(foo)][explicit name]]
#+end_src

      { oid = nilOid,

                        ("__block_type", "src"),
                        ("__src_language", "txt")

** Links to recognized web protocols

Links to web protocols like ~https://~ should be parsed as a ~LinkTargetURI~, but
not just for [[id:8fe81206-f50d-4200-a95e-99278e0ad410][Raw links]] but also those links found inside square brackets, like
~[[http://abc.xyz]]~ and ~[[http://abc.xyz][foo]]~.

#+name: f:protocolWebLinkParser
#+begin_src haskell
protocolWebLinkParser :: OrgParser Link
protocolWebLinkParser = do
  maybeUri <-
    MP.between
      (string "[[")
      (string "]")
      uriParser

  pState <- get
  let mask = pState.styleMask
      implicitName = maybeUri
      implicitNameStyledText =
        StyledText
          { body = implicitName,
            style = mask
          }
  displayName <-
    MP.choice
      [ MP.try $ string "]" >> pure [implicitNameStyledText],
        MP.try linkDisplayNameParser
      ]
  case N.parseURI (T.unpack maybeUri) of
    Nothing ->
      fail $ "(protocol web link) not a valid URI: " <> (T.unpack maybeUri)
    Just uri ->
      pure
        $ Link
          { target = LinkTargetURI uri,
            name = displayName
          }
  where
    uriParser :: OrgParser Text
    uriParser = do
      protocol <- MP.choice $ map (MP.try . string) protocolsWeb
      rest <- MP.takeWhile1P (Just "name character") (/= ']')
      pure $ protocol <> rest
#+end_src

#+name: ti:0006-links-web-protocols
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0006-links-web-protocols
#+begin_src org
[[http://foo.org]]
[[https://foo.org]]
[[https://foo.org][hello]]
[[mailto:x@foo.org][email]]
#+end_src

#+name: t:0006-links-web-protocols
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0006-links-web-protocols"
  ( nilOrgDoc
      [ CParagraph
          $ Prose
            [ PTokenLink
                Link
                  { target =
                      LinkTargetURI
                        N.URI
                          { N.uriScheme = "http:",
                            N.uriAuthority =
                              Just
                                N.URIAuth
                                  { N.uriUserInfo = "",
                                    N.uriRegName = "foo.org",
                                    N.uriPort = ""
                                  },
                            N.uriPath = "",
                            N.uriQuery = "",
                            N.uriFragment = ""
                          },
                    name = [stext "http://foo.org" []]
                  },
              ptext " " [],
              PTokenLink
                Link
                  { target =
                      LinkTargetURI
                        N.URI
                          { N.uriScheme = "https:",
                            N.uriAuthority =
                              Just
                                N.URIAuth
                                  { N.uriUserInfo = "",
                                    N.uriRegName = "foo.org",
                                    N.uriPort = ""
                                  },
                            N.uriPath = "",
                            N.uriQuery = "",
                            N.uriFragment = ""
                          },
                    name = [stext "https://foo.org" []]
                  },
              ptext " " [],
              PTokenLink
                Link
                  { target =
                      LinkTargetURI
                        N.URI
                          { N.uriScheme = "https:",
                            N.uriAuthority =
                              Just
                                N.URIAuth
                                  { N.uriUserInfo = "",
                                    N.uriRegName = "foo.org",
                                    N.uriPort = ""
                                  },
                            N.uriPath = "",
                            N.uriQuery = "",
                            N.uriFragment = ""
                          },
                    name = [stext "hello" []]
                  },
              ptext " " [],
              PTokenLink

                  { target =
                      LinkTargetURI
                        N.URI
                          { N.uriScheme = "mailto:",
                            N.uriAuthority = Nothing,
                            N.uriPath = "x@foo.org",
                            N.uriQuery = "",
                            N.uriFragment = ""
